where \(\lambda_i=\partial\lambda/\partial r_i\).

Thus the labelled-rate chart has covariance push-forward
\[
\Sigma_{\Gamma,\kappa}
=J_{\Gamma,\kappa}(r)\,\Sigma_r\,J_{\Gamma,\kappa}(r)^\top
\]
whenever a separate side-data hypothesis supplies a valid ordered chart and a
Gaussian limit for \(\widehat r_N\).  The visible record alone supplies only
the symmetric input \(J_\Phi(r)\) and the unordered roots; the ordered
labelled-rate stochastic statement is
\Cref{cor:side-data-labelled-rate-wald-main}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The implicit equation for a root is
\(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0\).  Differentiating gives
\[
(2z-\sigma_1)\,dz-z\,d\sigma_1+d\sigma_2=0.
\]
At separated roots \(2p-\sigma_1=p-s\) and \(2s-\sigma_1=s-p\), which gives
\eqref{eq:separated-root-jacobian-main}.  Thus the labelled-root differential
itself has a pole at \(p=s\); the quotient map \(\Phi\) remains the symmetric
coordinate map there, but the labelled-root and labelled-rate charts do not
extend through the exchange diagonal.  The logarithmic rate Jacobian is
the ordinary derivative of
\((p,s)\mapsto(-\log p/\Delta\tau,-\log s/\Delta\tau)\), proving
\eqref{eq:separated-rate-jacobian-main}.  The displayed formula for
\(\lambda=E/\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}\) is the quotient rule, and
\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main} follows by differentiating
\[
\Phi_1=1-a+\lambda,\qquad
\Phi_2=r_0-a+(1-r_0)\lambda .
\]
All denominators are bounded away from zero on the stated neighbourhood, so
the maps and plug-in Jacobians are continuous there.  The displayed covariance
push-forward is only the Jacobian sandwich for this externally supplied chart;
the probability limit and Wald statistic are stated separately in
\Cref{cor:side-data-labelled-rate-wald-main}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Fixed-setting retained-record asymptotics and the exchange diagonal}

The finite-record argument has three layers: the complete-cycle protocol, the
D-MAP-specific reward identities, and the quotient/projection consequences.
Every confidence statement in this subsection is conditional on the model
contract in \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main} and on the
pre-specified reporting gates stated below.  The gates are withholding rules
inside that fixed experiment; they are not tests that certify the model and
they do not make post-diagnostic branch choices coverage-valid.
The model-specific content in this subsection is the reduction of the
inclusion coordinates to the adjacent-gap rewards
\[
U_j^{(0)}=1,\qquad
U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},\qquad
U_j^{(2)}
=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,\ G_{j+1}=0\}},
\]
followed by the quotient inverse and the exchange-diagonal projection.  The
inverse statements are delta-method chart statements for identifiable
symmetric coordinates, with labelled rates only on a retained separated-root
branch satisfying \Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}.

\begin{assumption}[Standing retained-record experiment]\label{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}
All D-MAP finite-record statements in this subsection and in
\Cref{subsec:studentized-lag-two-main} are relative to the following single
record experiment, unless a statement explicitly says otherwise.  The model
and pointwise retained-record interpretation are those of
\Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}.  In the stationary version,
\((A_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) is the stationary visible binary process generated by
the contracted model kernels.  In the finite-start version, an arbitrary hidden
initial law is run through a deterministic burn-in \(L_N\to\infty\); the
observed record is the retained post-burn-in block of length \(N\), and every
complete-cycle index, batch, projection, Wald statistic, and finite-record report is
formed only from that retained visible word.  Equal-rate formulas are
interpreted by the continuous dictionary
\eqref{eq:equal-rate-dictionary-main}.  The same-bin recovery leakage
comparator excluded by
\Cref{prop:model-fragility-boundary-reset-main} is outside this retained-record
experiment; replacing the killed entries by positive one-labelled leakage would
require a different complete-cycle law, inverse map, and covariance estimator.
\end{assumption}

\paragraph{Generic regenerative tools.}
The model-independent renewal and separated-batch facts are collected in
\Cref{app:finite-record-regenerative-tools-main}.  They enter here only after
the killed-leakage renewal gap law has verified the required exponential tail
\cite{GlynnIglehart1990STS,AsmussenGlynn2007,
VatsFlegalJones2019Multivariate}.

The diagonal statistic below is not a likelihood-ratio statistic and the
feasible half-space is not estimated as a nuisance cone of a parametric
likelihood.  Its cone enters after the visible inclusion CLT is pushed through
the quotient inverse and then projected onto the local hypograph
\(\{h_2\le zh_1\}\).  The local projection step is proved below by the
explicit exchange-parabola normal equation, while Shapiro
metric-projection differentiability, Geyer constrained-estimation limits, and
Fang--Santos directional-delta inference remain the surrounding boundary and
directional-delta background
\cite{Shapiro1987MetricProjection,Geyer1994ConstrainedM,
FangSantos2019DirectionalDifferentiability}.  The classical boundary
likelihood-ratio theory of Chernoff and Self--Liang is still the relevant
nearby benchmark for cone inference and for the rank-one
\(\tfrac12\delta_0+\tfrac12\chi^2_1\) mixture, but here that mixture arises
from the retained projected-Gaussian quadratic form rather than from a
parametric cone LRT
\cite{Chernoff1954LikelihoodRatio,SelfLiang1987NonstandardLRT}.

The covariance estimator below is therefore a regenerative complete-cycle
estimator, not an MCMC theorem imported wholesale.  MCMC batch-means papers
supply useful terminology for long-run covariance estimation and retained-rank
numerical reporting, but the proof here uses the observed click cycles as the
regeneration blocks.

\begin{definition}[Cycle-index inclusion estimator]\label{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}
For a finite observed record \(A_0,\ldots,A_{N-1}\), write the complete click
epochs in the observed window as
\[
\tau_0<\tau_1<\cdots<\tau_{K_N},\qquad K_N=\max\{k:\tau_k<N\}.
\]
If the observed window contains no click, set \(K_N=-1\) and every
all-complete-cycle index set below to the empty set.  Thus \(K_N+1\) is the
number of observed clicks, and \(K_N\) is one less than the number of click
epochs from which complete observed cycles can be formed.  Set
\(L_j=\tau_{j+1}-\tau_j\) whenever both endpoints are observed.  For
\(m=0,1,2\), put
\[
X_n^{(m)}=A_nA_{n+m},\qquad X_n^{(0)}=A_n,
\]
and define the complete-cycle uncentered reward
\[
U_j^{(m)}=\sum_{n=\tau_j}^{\tau_{j+1}-1}X_n^{(m)}.
\]
Set \(c_0=c_1=0\) and \(c_2=1\).  The admissible all-complete-cycle index set is
\[
\begin{aligned}
J_{m,N}
=\{0\le j\le K_N-c_m-1:\;&
\tau_j,\tau_{j+1}\in[0,N-1],\\
&\text{and every }n+m\text{ entering }U_j^{(m)}
\text{ lies in }[0,N-1]\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The cycle-index estimator of the three inclusion coordinates
\[
\Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2)=(\PP(A_0=1),\PP(A_0=A_1=1),\PP(A_0=A_2=1))
\]
is
\[
\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\bigl(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
\widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\bigr)^\top,\qquad
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}U_j^{(m)} .
\]
The displayed sum is an operational estimator only after the relevant
complete-cycle set is nonempty.  Records with no click, too few complete
cycles, or no admissible terminal look-ahead cycles are not assigned the
numerical value \(0\) as an inferential estimate; the finite-record protocol
below instead stops with the message ``insufficient complete cycles; no
finite-record report.''  In asymptotic proofs only, we use the harmless auxiliary
extension in which an empty sum is interpreted as \(0\); that extension is not
part of the finite-record report.
For covariance estimation the three coordinates are evaluated on the common
complete-cycle set \(J_N=J_{0,N}\cap J_{1,N}\cap J_{2,N}\).  With plug-in
centering, the vector reward used in the separated batches is
\[
\widehat Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
U_j^{(1)}-\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
U_j^{(2)}-\widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j\bigr)^\top .
\]
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Master adjacent-gap covariance theorem.}
The covariance estimator used in Theorem~C is not assembled from several
asymptotic reductions.  It is the single adjacent-gap separated-batch object
below: the calendar stopping rule, the all-complete-cycle set \(J_N\), the
separator cycle, the empirical batch mean, the multiplier \(K_N/N\), and the
plug-in centering perturbation are all part of the same statement.
Theorems~C--E cite this result through the D-MAP reward identity and the
quotient sandwich statements below.

\begin{theorem}[Master adjacent-gap retained-cycle covariance with plug-in centering]\label{thm:main-calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin}
Let \((G_j)_{j\ge0}\) be i.i.d. nonnegative integer-valued gaps with
\(\EE e^{\eta G_0}<\infty\) for some \(\eta>0\), put \(L_j=G_j+1\),
\(\mu=\EE L_0\), and
\[
S_k=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}L_j,\qquad
K_N=\max\{k:S_k<N\}.
\]
Let \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\in\RR^d\) be a centered adjacent-gap reward:
\[
\EE Y_j=0,\qquad \EE\|Y_j\|^4<\infty .
\]
Define
\[
T_1=\Var(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
     +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma=T_1/\mu .
\]
The complete adjacent-gap retained cycle set is
\[
J_N=\{j:0\le j\le K_N-2\},
\]
with \(J_N=\varnothing\) if \(K_N<2\).  This is exactly the set of cycle
indices whose reward \(f(G_j,G_{j+1})\) and one-cycle look-ahead are complete
before the calendar stop \(N\).

For \(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\), put \(d_N=b_N+1\) and
\[
I_{\ell,N}=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\}.
\]
The omitted cycle \(\ell d_N-1\) is the separator between the retained
batches \(I_{\ell,N}\) and \(I_{\ell+1,N}\).  Define
\[
B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}Y_j
\]
for retained batches, and let the all-complete-cycle stopped batch count be
\[
M_N=\max\{M\ge0:I_{\ell,N}\subseteq J_N
        \text{ for every }1\le\ell\le M\}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
M_N=\max\{M\ge0:(M-1)(b_N+1)+b_N\le K_N-1\},
\]
with \(M_N=0\) if the set is empty.  On \(M_N\ge2\), write
\(\bar B_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}B_{\ell,N}\).  Then
\[
M_N=\frac{N}{\mu(b_N+1)}
    +O_{\PP}\!\left(\frac{\sqrt N}{b_N}+1\right),
\]
so \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one, and, with the harmless
auxiliary zero definition on \(M_N<2\),
\[
\left\|
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)^\top
-\Sigma
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
Moreover, if \(\Delta_N=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) in \(\RR^d\) and the reported
plug-in rewards are
\[
\widehat Y_j=Y_j-\Delta_NL_j,\qquad
\widehat B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}\widehat Y_j,\qquad
\overline{\widehat B}_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\widehat B_{\ell,N},
\]
then
\[
\max_{\ell\le M_N}\|\widehat B_{\ell,N}-B_{\ell,N}\|
=O_{\PP}(b_NN^{-1/2})
\]
and the centered quadratic perturbation obeys
\[
\left\|
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
\left\{
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)^\top
-(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)^\top
\right\}
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
Consequently the plug-in covariance estimator
\[
\widehat\Sigma_N:=
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)^\top
\]
satisfies, again with auxiliary zero completion on \(M_N<2\),
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_N-\Sigma\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
Thus, for \(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\),
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_N-\Sigma\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(N^{-\beta}+N^{-(1-\beta)/2}\right).
\]
For the protocol choice \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), the rate is
\(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The exponential moment gives the renewal and maximal-cycle estimates
\[
K_N=\frac{N}{\mu}+O_{\PP}(\sqrt N),\qquad
\max_{0\le j\le K_N+2}L_j=O_{\PP}(\log N).
\]
Thus the fixed one-cycle look-ahead deletion from
\(\{0,\ldots,K_N-1\}\) to \(J_N=\{0,\ldots,K_N-2\}\) removes only one cycle,
of calendar length \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\).  For the stopped batch count above,
\[
M_N=\frac{K_N}{b_N+1}+O(1)
=\frac{N}{\mu(b_N+1)}+O_{\PP}\!\left(\frac{\sqrt N}{b_N}+1\right),
\qquad
M_N\asymp_{\PP}\frac{N}{b_N}.
\]
In particular \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one.

The retained batch \(B_{\ell,N}\) depends only on
\[
G_{(\ell-1)d_N},\ldots,G_{(\ell-1)d_N+b_N}.
\]
The next retained batch starts at \(\ell d_N\).  The omitted separator index
\(\ell d_N-1\) is not batched, but it supplies the last gap
\(G_{\ell d_N-1}\) needed by the final reward in the previous batch; hence the
previous batch uses gaps only through \(G_{\ell d_N-1}\), while the next batch
uses gaps starting at \(G_{\ell d_N}\).  These gap sets are disjoint for
distinct \(\ell\), so the retained batch sums are independent.  Stationarity
and \(1\)-dependence give
\[
b_N^{-1}\Var(B_{\ell,N})=T_1+O(b_N^{-1}),
\]
where the \(O(b_N^{-1})\) term is the finite number of missing boundary
covariance layers inside a block.  Since \((Y_j)\) is \(1\)-dependent and
\(\EE\|Y_j\|^4<\infty\), the usual fourth-moment expansion for
\(1\)-dependent sums gives
\[
\EE\|B_{\ell,N}\|^4\le Cb_N^2 .
\]

Put
\[
X_{\ell,N}
=b_N^{-1}\{B_{\ell,N}B_{\ell,N}^\top
-\EE(B_{\ell,N}B_{\ell,N}^\top)\}.
\]
The preceding fourth-moment estimate gives
\(\sup_{\ell,N}\EE\|X_{\ell,N}\|_{\operatorname F}^2<\infty\).  Applying
Kolmogorov's maximal inequality entrywise to all deterministic prefixes
\(\ell\le C_1N/b_N\), and then using the stochastic bound on \(M_N\), yields
\[
\left\|
\frac1{M_N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}X_{\ell,N}
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
The empirical batch mean contributes only
\[
b_N^{-1}\bar B_N\bar B_N^\top
=O_{\PP}(M_N^{-1})
=O_{\PP}\left(\frac{b_N}{N}\right),
\]
and \(K_N/N=\mu^{-1}+O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Combining these displays proves the
oracle covariance bound.

For the plug-in rewards,
\[
\widehat B_{\ell,N}-B_{\ell,N}
=-\Delta_N\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}L_j .
\]
A Chernoff bound and a union bound over \(O_{\PP}(N/b_N)\) retained batches give
\[
\max_{\ell\le M_N}b_N^{-1}\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}L_j=O_{\PP}(1),
\]
so the displayed block perturbation follows from
\(\Delta_N=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Expanding the centered quadratic covariance in
\(\widehat B_{\ell,N}=B_{\ell,N}+(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-B_{\ell,N})\), the cross
term is \(O_{\PP}(\sqrt{b_N/N})\) by Cauchy's inequality and the oracle
covariance bound, while the pure plug-in quadratic term is
\(O_{\PP}(b_N/N)\).  Since \(b_N/N\le\sqrt{b_N/N}\) eventually, the plug-in
covariance has the same rate as the oracle covariance.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Inclusion-coordinate covariance estimation.}
Algorithm~1 reports \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\) only on the retained reporting
event; it does not use a zero matrix as an operational replacement.  Whenever
the main text writes an \(O_{\PP}\) or \(o_{\PP}\) statement for a covariance
matrix as a random element on the full probability space, the object is instead
the auxiliary completed matrix
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3}.
\]
This completion is only proof notation; on \(\mathcal E_N^c\) the finite-record
procedure withholds the covariance report and all finite-record reports.  The
master adjacent-gap theorem
\Cref{thm:main-calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin} is invoked only after
the following D-MAP reward identity has reduced the record coordinates to the
specific reward \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\).

\begin{proposition}[Exact inclusion-coordinate separated-batch covariance rate]\label{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main} and the deterministic
threshold protocol \ref{fr:protocol-main} at a population point in the stable
quotient chart \(r_0>0\), \(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\).  Let
\[
U_j=
\bigl(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\bigr)^\top,
\qquad
L_j=G_j+1,
\]
and put \(Y_j=U_j-\Rinc L_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\), where
\(\Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top\) and \(r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1)\).  Then
\(\EE Y_j=0\), \(\EE\|Y_j\|^4<\infty\), and \((Y_j)\) is \(1\)-dependent.
Let
\[
T_1^{(r)}
=\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
 +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma_r=T_1^{(r)}/\mu,\qquad \mu=\EE L_0 .
\]
Let \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\) be Algorithm~1's all-complete-cycle separated
batch covariance, formed from the plug-in rewards
\(\widehat Y_j=Y_j-(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)L_j\) with
\(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\).  Explicitly, with
\[
S_k=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}L_j,\qquad K_N=\max\{k:S_k<N\},\qquad
d_N=b_N+1,
\]
the separated batch blocks are
\[
I_{\ell,N}=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\},
\]
and the all-complete-cycle stopped batch count is
\[
M_N=\max\{M\ge0:(M-1)d_N+b_N\le K_N-1\},
\]
with \(M_N=0\) when the set is empty.  This is the same stopped count as
Algorithm~1's condition \(I_{\ell,N}\subseteq J_N\): the final batched reward
uses \(G_{j+1}\), so one terminal look-ahead complete cycle is required, and
the separator cycle between adjacent blocks is omitted.  With the auxiliary
completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3},
\]
one has
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=
O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
The displayed rate is for Algorithm~1's exact reported covariance object:
the random calendar-stopped prefix, fixed endpoint/look-ahead deletion,
empirical batch mean, multiplier \(K_N/N\), and plug-in centering by
\(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) are all included before the auxiliary
completion is introduced.
In particular, for the paper's fixed protocol
\[
b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor,
\]
the plug-in covariance estimator itself satisfies, on the all-complete-cycle
reported event and with
\(\overline{\widehat B}_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\widehat B_{\ell,N}\),
\[
\left\|
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac{1}{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)^\top
-\Sigma_r
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3}).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The displayed formula for \(U_j\) is the only model-specific input.  It is the
adjacent-gap form of the three inclusion rewards: a half-open Palm cycle
contains the click at its left endpoint, a lag-one inclusion occurs exactly on
\(G_j=0\), and a lag-two inclusion occurs on \(G_j=1\) or on two consecutive
zero gaps.  Palm inversion for these local windows gives
\(\EE U_j^{(m)}=r_m\EE L_j\), hence \(\EE Y_j=0\).  Because
\(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\) and the gaps are i.i.d., \((Y_j)\) is \(1\)-dependent,
and the exponential gap moment from
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main} gives the fourth moments
required by the master covariance theorem.

The concrete instantiation of
\Cref{thm:main-calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin} is
\[
d=3,\qquad T_1=T_1^{(r)},\qquad
\Delta_N=\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc .
\]
The common complete-cycle set
\(J_N=J_{0,N}\cap J_{1,N}\cap J_{2,N}\) and Algorithm~1's requirement that
every batch be contained in \(J_N\) are precisely the stopped condition
\((M-1)(b_N+1)+b_N\le K_N-1\).  Finally,
\Cref{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main} gives the plug-in scale
\(\Delta_N=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), and
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main} gives
\(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\).  Applying the master theorem with these
identifications proves the displayed rate for the reported covariance object,
including the empirical batch mean, the multiplier \(K_N/N\), the random
calendar-stopped batch count, and the plug-in rewards.  The auxiliary zero
completion has the same probability rate because the withholding event has
vanishing probability.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Off-diagonal gap-law formula for the inclusion covariance]\label{lem:off-diagonal-inclusion-covariance-gap-sums-main}
Work under the positive constrained D-MAP record model away from the exchange
diagonal, so that the two survival roots \(p\ne s\) are distinct.  Let
\[
g_k=\PP(G=k),\qquad k\ge0,
\]
be the Palm gap law, equivalently the off-diagonal closed law
\eqref{eq:symmetric-gap-law-main}.  Put
\[
\mu=\sum_{k\ge0}(k+1)g_k,\qquad
r=\frac1\mu(1,g_0,g_1+g_0^2)^\top .
\]
For \(i,j\ge0\), define the two-gap reward kernel
\[
u(i,j)=
\left(1,\mathbf1_{\{i=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{i=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{i=0,j=0\}}\right)^\top,
\qquad
y(i,j)=u(i,j)-r(i+1).
\]
Finally set
\[
\bar y(j):=\sum_{k\ge0}y(j,k)g_k
=
\left(
1-r_0(j+1),\
\mathbf1_{\{j=0\}}-r_1(j+1),\
\mathbf1_{\{j=1\}}+g_0\mathbf1_{\{j=0\}}-r_2(j+1)
\right)^\top .
\]
Then the cycle-time long-run covariance in
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main} has the explicit
gap-law representation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-covariance-gap-sums-main}
T_1^{(r)}=V_g+C_g+C_g^\top ,
\end{equation}
where
\begin{align}
V_g&=\sum_{i,j\ge0} y(i,j)y(i,j)^\top g_i g_j,
\label{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-var-gap-sum-main}\\
C_g&=\sum_{i,j\ge0} y(i,j)\bar y(j)^\top g_i g_j .
\label{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-adjacent-gap-sum-main}
\end{align}
Equivalently, entry by entry,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-entry-gap-sum-main}
[T_1^{(r)}]_{\alpha\beta}
=\sum_{i,j\ge0}y_\alpha(i,j)y_\beta(i,j)g_i g_j
+\sum_{i,j\ge0}
\{y_\alpha(i,j)\bar y_\beta(j)+\bar y_\alpha(j)y_\beta(i,j)\}g_i g_j .
\end{equation}
All finite-dimensional sums in
\eqref{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-var-gap-sum-main}--\eqref{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-entry-gap-sum-main}
are absolutely convergent.  Since the off-diagonal law
\eqref{eq:symmetric-gap-law-main} is a finite linear combination of
\(p^k\) and \(s^k\), these finite and double sums reduce, if desired, to
ordinary geometric moment sums; no Monte Carlo or covariance-tail truncation is
part of the definition.  The retained-record covariance in Theorem~C is
\[
\Sigma_r=\frac{T_1^{(r)}}{\mu}.
\]
Equivalently, let \(G_0,G_1,G_2\) be i.i.d. with law \(g\), put
\(L_i=G_i+1\), and set
\[
A=\EE\{u(G_0,G_1)u(G_0,G_1)^\top\},\qquad
B=\EE\{u(G_0,G_1)u(G_1,G_2)^\top\},
\]
\[
e=\EE\{L_0u(G_0,G_1)\},\qquad
d=\EE\{L_1u(G_0,G_1)\},\qquad
\nu=\EE L_0^2 .
\]
Then the same covariance is the reproducible matrix formula
\begin{equation}\label{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-covariance-ABednu-main}
\Sigma_r
=\frac1\mu\{A-er^\top-re^\top+\nu rr^\top
+B-dr^\top+B^\top-rd^\top\}.
\end{equation}
In summation form,
\[
A=\sum_{i,j\ge0}u(i,j)u(i,j)^\top g_i g_j,\qquad
B=\sum_{i,j,k\ge0}u(i,j)u(j,k)^\top g_i g_j g_k,
\]
\[
e=\sum_{i,j\ge0}(i+1)u(i,j)g_i g_j,\qquad
d=\sum_{i,j\ge0}(j+1)u(i,j)g_i g_j,\qquad
\nu=\sum_{i\ge0}(i+1)^2g_i .
\]
These sums also converge absolutely by the exponential gap tail.  Thus the
off-diagonal covariance is determined for every positive
\((\Gamma,\kappa_{\rm r},\Delta\tau)\) away from the exchange diagonal by
gap-law sums alone.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The complete-cycle reward identities in
\eqref{eq:inclusion-cycle-reward-identities-main} give
\[
Y_j=y(G_j,G_{j+1}).
\]
The Palm inversion identity used in
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main} gives
\(\EE Y_j=0\), and the displayed formula for \(r\) follows from
\[
\EE U_j=(1,g_0,g_1+g_0^2)^\top .
\]
Because the gaps are i.i.d.,
\[
\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)=\EE(Y_0Y_0^\top)
=\sum_{i,j\ge0}y(i,j)y(i,j)^\top g_i g_j=V_g .
\]
For the adjacent covariance,
\[
\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)=\EE(Y_0Y_1^\top)
=\sum_{i,j,k\ge0}y(i,j)y(j,k)^\top g_i g_jg_k .
\]
Summing first over the independent future gap \(k\) gives
\[
\sum_{k\ge0}y(j,k)g_k=\bar y(j),
\]
with the displayed closed expression; hence
\(\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)=C_g\).  Transposition gives
\(\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)=C_g^\top\), which proves
\eqref{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-covariance-gap-sums-main}.

The \(A,B,e,d,\nu\) display is the same identity expanded before centering.
Indeed,
\[
\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)
=\EE\{(u(G_0,G_1)-rL_0)(u(G_0,G_1)-rL_0)^\top\}
=A-er^\top-re^\top+\nu rr^\top .
\]
Also
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
&=\EE\{(u(G_0,G_1)-rL_0)(u(G_1,G_2)-rL_1)^\top\}\\
&=B-dr^\top-r\,\EE\{L_0u(G_1,G_2)^\top\}
  +rr^\top\EE(L_0L_1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Since \(G_0\) is independent of \((G_1,G_2)\),
\(\EE\{L_0u(G_1,G_2)\}=\mu\,\EE u(G_1,G_2)=\mu^2r\), and
\(\EE(L_0L_1)=\mu^2\).  These last two terms cancel, so
\(\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)=B-dr^\top\), and transposition gives
\(\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)=B^\top-rd^\top\).  Dividing
\(T_1^{(r)}\) by \(\mu\) proves
\eqref{eq:off-diagonal-inclusion-covariance-ABednu-main}.

The exponential gap bound in
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main} and the linear growth
of \(y(i,j)\) and \(\bar y(j)\) imply absolute convergence of all displayed
sums.  Finally, \eqref{eq:symmetric-gap-law-main} expresses \(g_k\) as a
finite linear combination of geometric sequences, so the required moments are
finite combinations of the usual geometric derivative sums.
\end{proof}

The next theorem records the D-MAP reward specialization and the retained-rank
consequence used by the main inferential theorems.

\begin{proposition}[D-MAP specialization of separated-batch covariance]\label{thm:finite-record-covariance-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main} and the deterministic
threshold protocol \ref{fr:protocol-main} at a population point in the stable
quotient chart \(r_0>0\), \(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\).
Let \(U_j\), \(Y_j\), \(T_1^{(r)}\), and
\(\Sigma_r=T_1^{(r)}/\mu\) be the adjacent-gap reward and calendar-time
covariance from
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main}.  Away from the
exchange diagonal, \(T_1^{(r)}\) is the explicit finite-gap-law expression
in \Cref{lem:off-diagonal-inclusion-covariance-gap-sums-main}; on the
exchange diagonal, the corresponding computable closed formula is
\Cref{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main}.  Algorithm~1
forms the separated all-complete-cycle plug-in covariance estimator
\(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\) and reports it only on \(\mathcal E_N\).  For
probability limits use the auxiliary total-space completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3}.
\]
With a deterministic separated batch length
\(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\), this completed matrix satisfies
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=
O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right)
=
O_{\PP}\left(N^{-\beta}+N^{-(1-\beta)/2}\right).
\]
With
\[
\omega_\beta:=\min\{\beta,(1-\beta)/2\},
\]
any rank-adaptive deterministic threshold \(t_N\) must satisfy
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N).
\]
A concrete admissible choice is
\[
t_N=N^{-\alpha},
\qquad
0<\alpha<\omega_\beta;
\]
for the balanced choice \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), this becomes any
\(0<\alpha<1/3\), for instance \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The D-MAP reward identity and the scale
\(\Sigma_r=T_1^{(r)}/\mu\) are those of
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main}.  That proposition
instantiates the master adjacent-gap theorem
\Cref{thm:main-calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin} with
\(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})=U_j-\Rinc L_j\) and
\(\Delta_N=\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Therefore
Algorithm~1 gives
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
Substituting \(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\), gives
\[
O_{\PP}\left(N^{-\beta}+N^{-(1-\beta)/2}\right).
\]
The threshold claim is immediate from Weyl's eigenvalue inequality: every
positive limiting eigenvalue stays above \(t_N\), and every zero limiting
eigenvalue falls below \(t_N\), with probability tending to one whenever
\(\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N)\).
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Conditional projected-Gaussian law at a moving half-space]\label{lem:projected-gaussian-bootstrap-halfspace-main}
For \(t\in(0,1)\), write
\[
H_t=\{h\in\RR^2:h_2\le th_1\}.
\]
Let \(z_N\to z\in(0,1)\) in probability and let
\(\widehat\Sigma_N\) be a symmetric nonnegative definite covariance estimator
such that
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_N-\Sigma\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\qquad t_N\downarrow0,
\]
for a fixed nonnegative definite \(2\times2\) matrix \(\Sigma\).  Let
\[
\widehat\Sigma_N^+
=\sum_{\widehat\lambda_{i,N}>t_N}
\widehat\lambda_{i,N}^{-1}
\widehat v_{i,N}\widehat v_{i,N}^{\top}
\]
be the thresholded Moore--Penrose inverse.  Suppose that
\[
X_N\Rightarrow X:=\Pi_{H_z}(Z),
\qquad Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma).
\]
This is the local constrained-estimation limit: the Gaussian perturbation is
projected onto the tangent half-space before the quadratic statistic is formed.
It is the same projected-normal structure that appears in constrained
\(M\)-estimation limits \cite{Geyer1994ConstrainedM}.  The simulation below is
therefore not an ordinary nonparametric or parametric bootstrap from the
record; it is the conditional plug-in projected Gaussian law, with retained
empirical rank.
Define
\[
T_N=X_N^\top\widehat\Sigma_N^+X_N
\]
and, conditionally on the observed record,
\[
Z_N^\ast\sim \mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_N),\qquad
X_N^\ast=\Pi_{H_{z_N}}(Z_N^\ast),\qquad
T_N^\ast=(X_N^\ast)^\top\widehat\Sigma_N^+X_N^\ast .
\]
Then
\[
T_N\Rightarrow T:=X^\top\Sigma^+X,
\]
and the conditional law of \(T_N^\ast\) converges weakly in probability to
the law of \(T\).  Consequently, if \(c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\) is the lower
conditional \((1-\alpha)\)-quantile of \(T_N^\ast\) and the limiting quantile
\[
c_{1-\alpha}:=\inf\{c:\PP(T\le c)\ge1-\alpha\}
\]
is a continuity point of the distribution function of \(T\), then
\[
c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\to c_{1-\alpha}
\quad\text{in probability}.
\]
If in addition \(\PP(T\le c_{1-\alpha})=1-\alpha\), then
\[
\PP\{T_N\le c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\}\to1-\alpha .
\]
If \(\Sigma=0\), then the retained-rank inverse is the zero matrix with
probability tending to one; consequently \(T_N=0\) and \(T_N^\ast=0\) with
probability tending to one, and the fixed positive-buffer lower-coverage
conclusion follows from
\Cref{lem:buffered-random-quantile-coverage-main}.

\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Weyl's inequality and the condition
\(\|\widehat\Sigma_N-\Sigma\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N)\) imply that,
with probability tending to one, every positive eigenvalue of \(\Sigma\) is
retained and every zero eigenvalue is removed by the threshold.  On that event
the retained spectral projection converges to the range projection of
\(\Sigma\), and
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_N^+-\Sigma^+\|_{\operatorname{op}}\to0
\]
in probability.  Slutsky's theorem therefore gives
\[
X_N^\top\widehat\Sigma_N^+X_N
\Rightarrow X^\top\Sigma^+X .
\]
In the special case \(\Sigma=0\), the same eigenvalue bound says
\[
\PP\{\|\widehat\Sigma_N\|_{\operatorname{op}}\le t_N\}\to1,
\]
so the thresholded retained-rank inverse is exactly zero with probability
tending to one.  Hence \(T_N=0\) and, conditionally, \(T_N^\ast=0\) on that
high-probability event.  The limiting distribution is the point mass at zero,
whose lower \(q\)-quantile is zero for every \(0<q<1\); the positive-buffer
coverage statement is therefore the buffered random-quantile conclusion.

The remaining issue is conditional Gaussian convergence after the moving
projection.  The half-space projection has the explicit formula
\[
\Pi_{H_t}(u)
=u-\frac{(u_2-tu_1)_+}{1+t^2}\binom{-t}{1}.
\]
Thus \((t,u)\mapsto\Pi_{H_t}(u)\) is continuous, uniformly for \(t\) in compact
subintervals of \((0,1)\) and \(u\) in bounded sets.  Since
\(\widehat\Sigma_N\to\Sigma\) in probability, the conditional Gaussian laws
\(\mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_N)\) converge weakly in probability to
\(\mathcal N(0,\Sigma)\).  Combining this Gaussian continuity with
\(z_N\to z\) and \(\widehat\Sigma_N^+\to\Sigma^+\) shows that, for every
bounded continuous \(\varphi:\RR\to\RR\),
\[
\EE^\ast\varphi(T_N^\ast)
\to
\EE\varphi\!\left((\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^+\Pi_{H_z}Z\right)
\]
in probability, where \(\EE^\ast\) denotes conditional expectation given the
record.  This is conditional weak convergence in probability.  The quantile
conclusion is the standard consequence of weak convergence at a continuity
point of the limiting distribution function; the final coverage statement
then follows from the convergence of \(T_N\) and the random quantile
\(c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Buffered random-quantile lower coverage]\label{lem:buffered-random-quantile-coverage-main}
Let \(T_N\Rightarrow T\), and let \(F_N^\ast\) be random conditional
distribution functions such that
\[
F_N^\ast(t)\to F(t):=\PP(T\le t)
\qquad\text{in probability}
\]
at every continuity point \(t\) of \(F\).  Fix \(0<q<1\), and define the
lower random conditional \(q\)-quantile and the limiting lower \(q\)-quantile
by
\[
c_N^-=\inf\{t:F_N^\ast(t)\ge q\},
\qquad
c^-=\inf\{t:F(t)\ge q\}.
\]
Assume \(c^-\) is finite.  Then, for every fixed \(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\liminf_{N\to\infty}\PP\{T_N\le c_N^-+\varepsilon\}\ge q .
\]
No continuity of \(F\) at \(c^-\), and no equality \(F(c^-)=q\), is required.
In particular, this lemma applies to the diagonal projected-Gaussian
zero-quantile alternatives after replacing \(q\) by \(1-\alpha\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
First the random lower quantile cannot fall a fixed distance below the
limiting lower quantile.  Fix \(\delta>0\).  By the definition of \(c^-\),
\(F(c^--\delta)<q\).  Choose a continuity point
\[
x\in(c^--\delta,c^-)
\]
with \(F(x)<q\); distribution functions have at most countably many
discontinuities, so such a point is available after decreasing \(x\) slightly
if needed.  The pointwise conditional convergence gives
\[
F_N^\ast(x)\to F(x)
\qquad\text{in probability}.
\]
For any distribution function \(G\), the event
\(\inf\{t:G(t)\ge q\}\le x\) implies \(G(x)\ge q\).  Hence
\[
\PP\{c_N^-\le x\}
\le
\PP\{F_N^\ast(x)\ge q\}\to0,
\]
and \(c_N^->x\) with probability tending to one.

Choose \(x>c^- -\varepsilon\), and then choose a continuity point
\[
y\in(c^-,x+\varepsilon).
\]
This is possible because \(x+\varepsilon>c^-\).  On the event
\(\{c_N^->x\}\), the inclusion
\[
\{T_N\le y\}\subseteq\{T_N\le c_N^-+\varepsilon\}
\]
holds.  Therefore
\[
\liminf_N\PP\{T_N\le c_N^-+\varepsilon\}
\ge
\liminf_N\PP\{T_N\le y\}.
\]
Weak convergence at the continuity point \(y\) gives
\[
\lim_N\PP\{T_N\le y\}=F(y).
\]
Finally \(y>c^-\) implies \(F(y)\ge q\) by the lower-quantile definition.
The displayed lower bound follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conditional Monte Carlo diagnostic and auxiliary domination]\label{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\) and condition on the observed record.  Let
\[
T_N^\ast(z)=
\Pi_{H_z}(Z_N^\ast)^\top\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}
\Pi_{H_z}(Z_N^\ast),
\qquad
Z_N^\ast\mid A_0,\ldots,A_{N-1}\sim
\mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}),
\]
where \(H_z=\{h:h_2\le zh_1\}\), and let
\[
F_N^\ast(t;z)=\PP^\ast\{T_N^\ast(z)\le t\}
\]
be its conditional distribution function.  If
\(T_{N,1}^\ast(z),\ldots,T_{N,R}^\ast(z)\) are conditionally independent draws
from this law and
\[
\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)
=\frac1R\sum_{m=1}^R\mathbf1_{\{T_{N,m}^\ast(z)\le t\}},
\]
then for every \(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\PP^\ast\!\left(
\sup_{t\in\mathbb R}
\left|\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)-F_N^\ast(t;z)\right|>\varepsilon
\right)
\le 2e^{-2R\varepsilon^2}.
\]
Equivalently, for every \(0<\delta<1\), with conditional probability at least
\(1-\delta\),
\[
\sup_t|\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)-F_N^\ast(t;z)|
\le
\varepsilon_{R,\delta}:=
\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2R}} .
\]
Let \(q_\alpha=1-\alpha\), let
\[
c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)
=\inf\{t:F_N^\ast(t;z)\ge q_\alpha\},
\qquad
\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z)
=T_{N,(\lceil q_\alpha R\rceil)}^\ast(z),
\]
where the draws are sorted increasingly.  If \(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\) is a
continuity point of \(F_N^\ast(\cdot;z)\), then
\[
\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z)
\longrightarrow c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)
\qquad\text{in conditional probability as }R\to\infty .
\]
If the conditional law has an atom or a flat stretch at the lower quantile,
the raw order statistic still estimates a lower conditional quantile in the
usual empirical-quantile sense, but the raw order statistic at level
\(q_\alpha\) is not by itself a one-sided coverage certificate.  A DKW
certificate is obtained by spending probability in the distribution-function
scale.  If
\[
\varepsilon_{R,\delta}<\alpha,\qquad
q_{\alpha,\delta,R}^{+}:=q_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta}<1,
\]
put \(\widehat c_R=\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z)\) and define
\[
d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z)
:=
\inf\left\{d\ge0:
\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(\widehat c_R+d;z)-\varepsilon_{R,\delta}
\ge 1-\alpha\right\}.
\]
Equivalently, since the empirical distribution is a step function, the
inflated lower empirical quantile is
\[
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)
:=T_{N,(\lceil q_{\alpha,\delta,R}^{+}R\rceil)}^\ast(z),
\]
and \(\widehat c_R+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z)
=\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)\).
Then, conditionally on the observed record,
\[
\PP^\ast\!\left\{
\widehat c_R+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z)
\ge c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\right\}\ge1-\delta .
\]
Equivalently, the inflated simulated critical value is the empirical lower
\((q_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta})\)-quantile.  This conclusion is
distribution-free and does not require continuity or a positive density at the
ideal conditional quantile.

For the record-probability consequence, fix the true diagonal coordinate
\(z_0\).  Throughout this paragraph \(\PP\) is the fixed-population law of the
observed retained record and all record-level gates, whereas \(\PP^\ast\) is
only the conditional law of the auxiliary Gaussian simulation after that
record has been observed.  The DKW step below is not a finite-sample
statement about record coverage; it is a conditional one-sided domination
statement for the simulated projected-Gaussian critical value.  Let
\(G_N=G_{\mathrm{cert},N}(z_0)\) be the record-level reporting event on which
the population branch, ideal-quantile enclosure, and numerical recipe are
certified.  A finite report may set \(G_N=1\) only when the manuscript or
attached transcript contains the branch certificate and the ideal conditional
quantile enclosure used by the reported inequality; otherwise the event is
declared false for coverage-certification purposes.  Let
\[
H_N=G_{\mathrm{MC},N}(z_0)
=\{\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)
+d_{R,\delta_N^{\rm MC},N}(z_0)\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\}
\]
be the auxiliary one-sided Monte Carlo domination event.  For each realized
record, \(H_N\) is an event in the auxiliary simulation space; its probability
is measured by \(\PP^\ast(\,\cdot\,)\), not by \(\PP\).  The event \(H_N\) is
part of the report only through a displayed one-sided certificate, for example
the DKW event and inflation in the preceding display.  Suppose
\[
\PP(G_N)\to1,\qquad \delta_N^{\rm MC}\to0,
\]
and on \(G_N\),
\[
\PP^\ast\{\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)
+d_{R,\delta_N^{\rm MC},N}(z_0)\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\}
\ge1-\delta_N^{\rm MC} .
\]
Define the auxiliary all-space fixed-candidate ideal and simulated diagonal
acceptance events by
\[
\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{aux}}(z_0)
:=\{Q_N(z_0)\le c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\},
\]
and
\[
\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC,aux}}(z_0)
:=\{Q_N(z_0)\le
\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)+d_{R,\delta_N^{\rm MC},N}(z_0)\}.
\]
The operational reported events are
\[
\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{rep}}(z_0)
:=\mathcal E_N\cap\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{aux}}(z_0),
\qquad
\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC,rep}}(z_0)
:=\mathcal E_N\cap\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC,aux}}(z_0).
\]
On \(\mathcal E_N^c\) the finite-record procedure withholds the diagonal
report, so no diagonal finite-record report is formed there.
Then
\[
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC,rep}}(z_0)\}
\ge
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{aux}}(z_0)\}
-\delta_N^{\rm MC}-\PP(G_N^c)-\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)-o(1).
\]
Therefore, if the ideal event has limiting coverage \(1-\alpha\), the simulated
reported event has
\[
\liminf_N
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC,rep}}(z_0)\}
\ge1-\alpha .
\]
Without such an explicit event \(G_{\mathrm{MC},N}(z_0)\) and one-sided
inflation \(d_{R,\delta_N^{\rm MC},N}(z_0)\), finite simulation remains only a
numerical diagnostic for the ideal conditional projected-Gaussian law.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Conditional on the record, the variables
\(T_{N,1}^\ast(z),\ldots,T_{N,R}^\ast(z)\) are independent real-valued draws
with distribution function \(F_N^\ast(\cdot;z)\).  The displayed exponential
bound is therefore the Dvoretzky--Kiefer--Wolfowitz inequality, in Massart's
sharp-constant form, applied under \(\PP^\ast\) \cite{Massart1990DKW}.

Let
\[
\Delta_{N,R}(z)=
\sup_t|\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)-F_N^\ast(t;z)|.
\]
The DKW bound gives \(\Delta_{N,R}(z)=O_{\PP^\ast}(R^{-1/2})\).  At a
continuity point of the conditional quantile, the Glivenko--Cantelli
consequence of this bound gives the standard empirical-quantile consistency:
for every \(\eta>0\), after decreasing \(\eta\) if necessary on the left,
\[
F_N^\ast(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)-\eta;z)<q_\alpha
\le F_N^\ast(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)+\eta;z).
\]
Uniform convergence of \(\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast\) to \(F_N^\ast\) then forces
the order statistic into this \(\eta\)-neighbourhood with conditional
probability tending to one.  At a discontinuity or flat quantile the same DKW
bound controls the simulated distribution function but the uninflated
\(q_\alpha\)-order statistic need not dominate the ideal lower quantile.

The DKW-inflated statistic gives a one-sided certificate without any local
regularity.  On the event
\[
B_{R,\delta,N}(z)=
\left\{\Delta_{N,R}(z)\le\varepsilon_{R,\delta}\right\},
\]
we have, with
\[
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)
=T_{N,(\lceil(q_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta})R\rceil)}^\ast(z),
\]
that
\[
\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast
\bigl(\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z);z\bigr)
\ge q_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta}.
\]
Therefore
\[
F_N^\ast
\bigl(\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z);z\bigr)
\ge q_\alpha .
\]
By the definition of the lower quantile,
\[
c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)
\le
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)
=\widehat c_R
+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z).
\]
Since the DKW inequality gives \(\PP^\ast(B_{R,\delta,N}(z))\ge1-\delta\),
the stated DKW domination bound follows.  The only deterministic size
condition needed for this construction is
\(\varepsilon_{R,\delta}<\alpha\), which ensures that the empirical
\((q_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta})\)-quantile is below level one.

It remains to prove the coverage-transfer inequality.
On \(G_N\cap H_N\), the auxiliary ideal fixed-candidate event is contained in the
simulated event, because the simulated critical value is at least as large as
the ideal one.  Intersecting this inclusion with the reporting gate
\(\mathcal E_N\) and then taking conditional expectation over the auxiliary
Monte Carlo draws gives
\[
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC,rep}}(z_0)\}
\ge
\PP\{\mathcal E_N\cap\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{aux}}(z_0)\cap G_N\}
-\EE\{1_{G_N}\PP^\ast(H_N^c)\}.
\]
The assumed conditional bound on \(G_N\) makes the last term at most
\(\delta_N^{\rm MC}\).  Since
\[
\PP\{\mathcal E_N\cap\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{aux}}(z_0)\cap G_N\}
\ge
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{aux}}(z_0)\}
-\PP(G_N^c)-\PP(\mathcal E_N^c),
\]
the displayed inequality follows, in fact without the harmless \(o(1)\) term
except for any asymptotically negligible convention used to make the finite
Monte Carlo rule measurable on nonreporting records.  The final \(\liminf\)
claim follows from \(\PP(G_N^c)\to0\), \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\),
\(\delta_N^{\rm MC}\to0\), and the assumed ideal coverage limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[DKW simulation domination on the auxiliary product space]\label{cor:dkw-product-space-diagonal-main}
Fix the certified exchange-diagonal candidate \(z_0\) and condition on the
retained record.  Let \(P_N\) denote the record law and let \(P_N^\ast\) denote
the conditional law of the projected-Gaussian simulation used to approximate
the conditional law of \(T_N^\ast(z_0)\).  Draw \(N\) auxiliary values
\(T^\ast_1,\ldots,T^\ast_N\) from this conditional law and sort them
increasingly.  Define
\[
\epsilon_N^{MC}=\sqrt{\frac{\log(2N^2)}{2N}}, \qquad
c_{N,DKW}=T^\ast_{(\lceil(1-\alpha+\epsilon_N^{MC})N\rceil)} .
\]
When \(\epsilon_N^{MC}<\alpha\),
\[
P_N^\ast\{c_{N,DKW}\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}\}\ge1-N^{-2},
\]
where \(c^-_{1-\alpha,N}\) is the ideal lower conditional
\((1-\alpha)\)-quantile for the same retained projected-Gaussian branch.
Therefore, with
\[
A_N^{aux}:=\{Q_N\le c^-_{1-\alpha,N}\},
\]
the product-space record-and-simulation statement is
\[
(P_N\otimes P_N^\ast)\{E_N\cap[Q_N\le c_{N,DKW}]\}
\ge
P_N(A_N^{aux})-P_N(E_N^c)-N^{-2}-o(1).
\]
This is only a domination statement for the auxiliary simulated critical
value.  It is not a bootstrap from the observed record and is not finite-sample
record coverage; record coverage enters only through the separate fixed-null
asymptotic theorem for \(A_N^{aux}\) and the retained reporting gate \(E_N\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The first display is \Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main} with
\(R=N\), \(\delta=N^{-2}\), and
\(\epsilon_N^{MC}=\sqrt{\log(2N^2)/(2N)}\).  On the auxiliary domination event
\(\{c_{N,DKW}\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}\}\), the ideal event
\(A_N^{aux}\) implies \(\{Q_N\le c_{N,DKW}\}\).  Therefore
\[
1_{E_N}1_{A_N^{aux}}1_{\{c_{N,DKW}\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}\}}
\le
1_{E_N}1_{\{Q_N\le c_{N,DKW}\}} .
\]
Taking first \(P_N^\ast\)-expectation conditional on the record and then
\(P_N\)-expectation gives
\[
(P_N\otimes P_N^\ast)\{E_N\cap[Q_N\le c_{N,DKW}]\}
\ge
P_N(E_N\cap A_N^{aux})-N^{-2}-o(1),
\]
where the harmless \(o(1)\) allows the usual measurable completion on records
where the finite reporting protocol withholds the simulation transcript.
Finally
\[
P_N(E_N\cap A_N^{aux})\ge P_N(A_N^{aux})-P_N(E_N^c),
\]
which proves the displayed inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Deterministic full-rank projected-Gaussian quantile]\label{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\), let
\[
H_z=\{u\in\mathbb R^2:u_2\le zu_1\},\qquad
n_z=(-z,1)^\top,
\]
and let \(Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma)\), where \(\Sigma\) is a positive
definite \(2\times2\) matrix.  For \(c\in\mathbb R\), define
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=
\PP\{Z\in H_z,\ Z^\top\Sigma^{-1}Z\le c\}
+\PP\{Z\notin H_z,\ 
(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}Z)\le c\}.
\]
Equivalently, with Gaussian density \(\varphi_\Sigma\) and with
\[
\Pi_{H_z}u=
\begin{cases}
u,&n_z^\top u\le0,\\[0.2em]
u-\dfrac{n_z^\top u}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z,&n_z^\top u>0,
\end{cases}
\]
the same function is the deterministic integral
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(c)
=\int_{H_z}
\mathbf1_{\{u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\le c\}}\varphi_\Sigma(u)\,du
+\int_{H_z^c}
\mathbf1_{\{(\Pi_{H_z}u)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}u)\le c\}}
\varphi_\Sigma(u)\,du .
\]
It is the distribution function of
\[
T_{z,\Sigma}=(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}Z).
\]
Hence \(F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=0\) for \(c<0\), \(F_{z,\Sigma}\) is nondecreasing
and right-continuous, and \(\lim_{c\to\infty}F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=1\).  For
\(0<q<1\), the deterministic lower projected-Gaussian quantile is
\[
c_q(z,\Sigma):=\inf\{c\ge0:F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\ge q\}.
\]
This number is finite and satisfies
\[
\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}\le c_q(z,\Sigma)\}\ge q,\qquad
\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}<c_q(z,\Sigma)\}\le q.
\]
Moreover \(F_{z,\Sigma}\) has no atoms; in particular
\(\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}=c_q(z,\Sigma)\}=0\) and
\(\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}\le c_q(z,\Sigma)\}=q\).  It is also strictly
increasing on \([0,\infty)\): if \(0\le a<b\), then
\(F_{z,\Sigma}(b)>F_{z,\Sigma}(a)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The displayed projection formula is the Euclidean projection onto the closed
half-space \(H_z\), so the two integrals partition the sample space into the
inside and outside events and give exactly the distribution function of
\(T_{z,\Sigma}\).  Distribution functions are nondecreasing and
right-continuous.  Since the quadratic form is nonnegative and finite almost
surely, \(F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=0\) for \(c<0\) and
\(\lim_{c\to\infty}F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=1\), which makes the displayed lower
quantile finite for every \(0<q<1\).  The two lower-quantile inequalities are
the standard consequences of the definition of
\(\inf\{c:F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\ge q\}\) and right-continuity.

It remains only to record why no atom is hidden in the projection.  On \(H_z\),
the statistic is the nonzero quadratic polynomial \(u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\).  On
\(H_z^c\), the projection is the linear map
\[
L_z u=u-\frac{n_z^\top u}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z
\]
and the statistic is the nonzero quadratic polynomial
\(u^\top L_z^\top\Sigma^{-1}L_z u\).  For each fixed \(c\), the corresponding
level set in either half-plane has two-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero:
for \(c>0\) it is contained in a conic section, and for \(c=0\) it is a line
or the origin.  The full-rank Gaussian law has a Lebesgue density, so
\(\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}=c\}=0\) for every \(c\).  Therefore the lower quantile is
an exact \(q\)-quantile.  To see strict increase, fix \(0\le a<b\).  The
half-space \(H_z\) has nonempty interior, and the open set
\[
\{u\in\operatorname{int}H_z:a<u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u<b\}
\]
is nonempty.  On this set \(T_{z,\Sigma}=u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\), and the
full-rank Gaussian density is strictly positive.  Hence
\(\PP\{a<T_{z,\Sigma}<b\}>0\), which proves the claimed strict increase.
\end{proof}

\ifdefined\SUPPLEMENTARYFULLPROTOCOLS
\begin{algorithmiclemma}[Transcript certificate for a finite diagonal comparison]\label{thm:certified-diagonal-inference-main}\label{prop:projected-gaussian-certificate-interface-main}
Fix \(0<\alpha<1\), put \(q_\alpha=1-\alpha\), and fix a reported exchange-diagonal
candidate \(z_\star\in(0,1)\).  A theorem-level finite diagonal comparison
certificate for this candidate is the single finite tuple
\[
\mathcal C(z_\star,\alpha,N)
=\bigl([z],[\theta],[\Sigma_\sigma],[I_\tau],
[I_{\det}],[w],[L,U],[Q]\bigr),
\]
together with the branch-specific auxiliary interval \([q]\) or
\([a_{\rm rn}]\) only in the rank-one non-normal case.  The tuple is mandatory:
without the branch entries, the critical-value bracket, and the statistic
enclosure in the same displayed transcript, the article has only an
asymptotic theorem or a diagnostic calculation, not a finite transcript
validating a realized accept/reject/undecided output.  The tuple entries have
the following meanings:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.4em,itemsep=0.25em]
\item a point or interval input enclosure \([z]\Subset(0,1)\) with
\(z_\star\in[z]\), and an outward-rounded enclosure \([\theta]\) of
\(\theta=-\log z_\star\);
\item outward-rounded interval enclosures for the entries of the finite
D-MAP covariance
\[
\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star)
=D\Phi(\Rinc(z_\star))\Sigma_r(z_\star)D\Phi(\Rinc(z_\star))^\top
\]
from \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main};
\item the branch intervals
\[
[I_\tau]\supset\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star),\qquad
[I_{\det}]\supset\det\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star),\qquad
[w]\supset\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star)(1,z_\star)^\top,
\]
and, when needed for the rank-one non-normal branch, the scale enclosure
\([a_{\rm rn}]\) of
\eqref{eq:diagonal-rank-one-nonnormal-scale-certificate-main};
\item an explicit rank margin \(\eta>0\) proving exactly one of the branch
alternatives in \Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main};
\item an outward-rounded statistic enclosure \([Q]=[[Q]^-,[Q]^+]\) with
\(Q(z_\star)\in[Q]\);
\item a certified critical-value bracket \([L,U]\), \(L<U\), for the same
branch and the same candidate, obtained either from the certified ideal-CDF
route or from a one-sided DKW simulation route.
\end{enumerate}
Thus all branch, quantile, and statistic comparisons are made by the single
object \(\mathcal C(z_\star,\alpha,N)\); algorithms may produce its entries,
but no algorithmic intermediate is itself a theorem-level certificate unless
it appears in this tuple or in the stated rank-one non-normal addendum.  In
particular, a printed population branch certificate without \([L,U]\) and
\([Q]\) does not license a finite comparison for a realized record.  The branch
item is passed only by one of the four mutually exclusive tests:
\[
[I_{\det}]\subset[\eta,\infty)\quad\text{(full rank),}
\]
\[
[I_{\det}]=[0,0],\quad [I_\tau]\subset[\eta,\infty),\quad
[w]=\{0\}
\quad\text{(rank one, normal range),}
\]
\[
[I_{\det}]=[0,0],\quad [I_\tau]\subset[\eta,\infty),\quad
[q]\subset[\eta,\infty),\quad [a_{\rm rn}]\Subset(0,1]
\quad\text{(rank one, non-normal range),}
\]
or exact entrywise \(\Sigma_\sigma(z_\star)=0\) for the zero-covariance
branch.  If none of these finite tests is certified, the transcript status is
\[
\text{\rm undecided: branch not certified}.
\]

For an ideal-CDF transcript, let \(F_{z_\star,\Sigma}\) denote the
projected-Gaussian CDF for the certified branch covariance \(\Sigma\), using
the full-rank formula of
\Cref{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}, the
rank-one non-normal formula
\eqref{eq:rank-one-nonnormal-cdf-main}, the rank-one normal mixture in
\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}, or the
zero-covariance degenerate law as appropriate.  The critical-value transcript
must contain outward-rounded CDF enclosures satisfying
\[
F^-(c)\le F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(c)\le F^+(c)
\]
at its displayed rational endpoints and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:certified-diagonal-critical-bracket-main}
F^+(L)<q_\alpha\le F^-(U).
\end{equation}
In the full-rank submitted branch this means explicitly that the same tuple
\(\mathcal C(z_\star,\alpha,N)\) proves
\[
[I_{\det}]\subset[\eta,\infty),
\qquad
F_N^+(L)<q_\alpha\le F_N^-(U),
\qquad
Q(z_\star)\in[Q],
\]
where \(F_N^\pm\) are the displayed outward-rounded CDF enclosures for the
retained full-rank projected-Gaussian law.  The finite comparison is then
performed only against the retained bracket endpoints \(L,U\), not against an
unbracketed floating-point quantile.
Then the exact lower projected-Gaussian critical value
\[
c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma):=\inf\{c:F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(c)\ge q_\alpha\}
\]
satisfies
\[
L<c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\le U.
\]
Consequently the finite theorem-level transcript validates exactly the
following comparison:
\[
[Q]^+\le L
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\text{\rm transcript-valid accept of } Q(z_\star)\le c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma),
\]
\[
[Q]^->U
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\text{\rm transcript-valid reject of } Q(z_\star)\le c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma),
\]
and in every remaining case
\[
\text{\rm transcript-valid undecided: } Q(z_\star)-c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)
\in [[Q]^--U,\,[Q]^+-L].
\]
A midpoint \(m\in(L,U)\) for which \(F^-(m)<q_\alpha\le F^+(m)\) is therefore only
an undecided midpoint inside a retained critical-value bracket; it is not an
equality certificate and not an accept/reject decision.  This transcript
validation theorem is separate from the fixed-null probability theorem
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}: Theorem~E supplies an
asymptotic ideal conditional critical value, while
\(\mathcal C(z_\star,\alpha,N)\) is the additional finite record-specific
evidence needed before any realized comparison can be printed.

For a DKW simulation transcript, the supplied critical value \(\widehat c\)
must satisfy the displayed one-sided auxiliary domination statement
\[
\PP^\ast\{\widehat c\ge c^{-}_{q_\alpha,N}(z_\star)\}\ge1-\delta_N,
\]
where \(c^{-}_{q_\alpha,N}(z_\star)\) is the ideal lower conditional
projected-Gaussian quantile for the same retained branch.  Conditional on that
auxiliary domination event, the exact finite transcript implication is
\[
[Q]^+\le\widehat c\Rightarrow\text{\rm transcript-valid DKW accept},\qquad
[Q]^->\widehat c\Rightarrow\text{\rm transcript-valid DKW reject},
\]
and otherwise \(\text{\rm undecided}\).  The probability attached to this
route is only the stated auxiliary \(P_N^\ast\) domination probability; record
coverage is obtained only by combining that product-space auxiliary statement
with the fixed-null asymptotic event of
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}.

If the candidate is not pre-specified, if \(z_\star\notin[z]\), if any branch
interval or rank margin is absent, if the critical-value bracket is for a
different candidate or branch, or if \([Q]\) is missing, the transcript status
is only
\[
\text{\rm undecided: no certified finite diagonal inference}.
\]
In the submitted manuscript the only attached paper-level branch certificate
is the singleton certificate \(z_\star=1/2\) in
\Cref{lem:z-half-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}; all other diagonal
candidate displays are diagnostics unless they attach their own finite
certificate data satisfying this theorem.  In particular, the singleton
branch transcript in
\Cref{app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main} is not this tuple:
it has \([z]\), \([\theta]\), \([\Sigma_\sigma]\), \([I_\tau]\),
\([I_{\det}]\), and \([w]\), but deliberately has no record-specific
\([L,U]\) and no realized statistic enclosure \([Q]\).
\end{algorithmiclemma}

\begin{proof}
The branch portion is exactly the finite rank/range classification certified
by \Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main} and justified by
\Cref{lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main,lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main}.
The interval enclosures contain the actual covariance and branch quantities,
and the margin \(\eta\) or exact zero identities select one population branch.
Without such a branch, the projected-Gaussian law used by the critical-value
calculation is not certified, so no accept/reject implication is licensed.

Assume first that the ideal-CDF transcript is attached.  From
\eqref{eq:certified-diagonal-critical-bracket-main},
\[
F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(L)\le F^+(L)<q_\alpha
\le F^-(U)\le F_{z_\star,\Sigma}(U).
\]
By the definition of the lower quantile this implies
\[
L<c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\le U.
\]
Since \(Q(z_\star)\in[Q]\), the implication
\([Q]^+\le L\) gives \(Q(z_\star)<c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\), and the implication
\([Q]^->U\) gives \(Q(z_\star)>c_{q_\alpha}(z_\star,\Sigma)\).  If neither separation
holds, the certified information only bounds the difference by
\([[Q]^--U,[Q]^+-L]\), which may contain zero; hence the only rigorous finite
transcript output is undecided.  The certified CDF and quantile bracketing routines used
to produce such endpoint inequalities are
\Cref{thm:certified-projected-gaussian-quantile-algorithm-main,lem:certified-deterministic-projected-gaussian-quantile-main,lem:certified-rank-one-nonnormal-projected-gaussian-quantile-main};
the rank-one normal and zero-covariance cases use the explicit laws stated in
\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}.

For the simulated route, the displayed DKW statement is a one-sided
certificate that the simulated critical value dominates the ideal conditional
critical value on an auxiliary event of probability at least
\(1-\delta_N\).  On that auxiliary event, the same interval comparison of
\([Q]\) with the supplied critical value proves the stated transcript-valid
accept, reject, or undecided implication.  The domination probability is conditional on the
auxiliary Gaussian simulation, so its record-level interpretation is exactly
the product-space statement proved in
\Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}, combined separately with
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}; it is not a finite-sample
coverage claim for the retained record alone.
\end{proof}
\else
\begin{remark}[Supplementary finite-comparison certificate interface]\label{thm:certified-diagonal-inference-main}\label{prop:projected-gaussian-certificate-interface-main}
The finite tuple that would be needed for a realized diagonal
accept/reject/undecided comparison is supplementary implementation material,
not part of the delivered main theorem chain.  The main article uses only the
population branch certificate at \(z_0=1/2\) and the ideal fixed-null
asymptotics of Theorem~E.  It prints no record-specific critical-value bracket
\([L,U]\), no statistic enclosure \([Q]\), and no complete finite comparison
tuple.
\end{remark}
\fi

\begin{lemma}[Projected-Gaussian continuity and rank degeneracy]\label{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\), put
\[
n_z=(-z,1)^\top,\qquad H_z=\{h:n_z^\top h\le0\},
\]
and let
\[
T_z=(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^+\Pi_{H_z}Z,
\qquad Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma),
\]
where \(\Sigma\) is a nonnegative definite \(2\times2\) matrix.  Then the
distribution of \(T_z\) is continuous in the following cases:
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=2,
\qquad\text{or}\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=1
\text{ and }
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma\ne \operatorname{span}\{n_z\}.
\]
If \(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=1\) and
\(\operatorname{Range}\Sigma=\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\), then
\[
\PP(T_z=0)=\frac12
\]
and \(T_z\) has no atom on \((0,\infty)\).  More precisely,
\[
T_z\sim \frac12\delta_0+\frac12\chi^2_1 .
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\PP(T_z=0)=\frac12,\qquad
\PP(T_z\le t)=\frac12+\frac12F_{\chi^2_1}(t),\quad t>0.
\]
Consequently, for a lower quantile level \(0<p_c<1\), the lower
\(p_c\)-quantile is
\[
c_{p_c}=0\quad\text{if }p_c\le\frac12,\qquad
c_{p_c}=F_{\chi^2_1}^{-1}(2p_c-1)\quad\text{if }p_c>\frac12,
\]
and the positive-quantile case is a continuity point of the law of \(T_z\).  If
\(\Sigma=0\), then
\(T_z=0\) almost surely.  Consequently the continuity condition in
\Cref{lem:projected-gaussian-bootstrap-halfspace-main} is automatic except
for the zero covariance case and for the rank-one case in which the retained
Gaussian direction is normal to the exchange-diagonal tangent half-space; in
that rank-one normal case the only discontinuity is the atom at zero.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If \(\Sigma=0\), the assertion is immediate.  Suppose first that
\(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=2\).  The Gaussian law has a Lebesgue density on
\(\RR^2\).  On \(H_z\), the statistic is the nonzero quadratic form
\(u^\top\Sigma^+u\).  On \(H_z^c\), the projection is the linear map
\[
L_z u=u-\frac{n_z^\top u}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z
\]
onto the boundary line \(n_z^\top h=0\), and the statistic is the quadratic
form \(u^\top L_z^\top\Sigma^+L_z u\), not identically zero on that half-plane.
For each fixed \(c\), the level set of either nonzero quadratic polynomial has
Lebesgue measure zero.  Hence \(\PP(T_z=c)=0\) for every \(c\).

Now let \(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=1\).  Write
\(\Sigma=\lambda vv^\top\), where \(\lambda>0\) and \(\|v\|=1\).  Then
\(Z=\xi v\), with \(\xi\sim\mathcal N(0,\lambda)\), and
\[
\Sigma^+=\lambda^{-1}vv^\top .
\]
If \(n_z^\top v=0\), the line
\(\operatorname{span}\{v\}\) lies in the boundary of \(H_z\), so
\(T_z=\xi^2/\lambda\), which has a continuous \(\chi^2_1\) law.  If
\(n_z^\top v\ne0\), one half-line is already in \(H_z\), where again
\(T_z=\xi^2/\lambda\).  On the other half-line,
\[
\Pi_{H_z}(\xi v)=
\xi\left(v-\frac{n_z^\top v}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z\right).
\]
The corresponding coefficient in the seminorm \(\Sigma^+\) is
\[
\lambda^{-1}
\left\{v^\top\left(v-\frac{n_z^\top v}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z\right)\right\}^2 .
\]
This coefficient is positive unless \(v\) is parallel to \(n_z\).  Therefore,
when \(\operatorname{Range}\Sigma\ne\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\), \(T_z\) is
a two-sided mixture of positive multiples of \(\xi^2\) and has no atoms.  When
  \(\operatorname{Range}\Sigma=\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\), the outside
  half-line projects to the origin and contributes mass \(1/2\) at zero, while
  the inside half-line gives \(T_z=\xi^2/\lambda\).  Conditional on this
  half-line, \(\xi^2/\lambda\) has the \(\chi^2_1\) law.  Hence the distribution
  is \(\frac12\delta_0+\frac12\chi^2_1\), and the stated lower-quantile split
  follows immediately from its distribution function
  \(F(t)=1/2+\PP\{\chi^2_1\le t\}/2\) for \(t>0\).  This proves the rank-one
  alternatives.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Computable diagonal covariance]\label{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main}
Work at a fixed constrained D-MAP exchange-diagonal population point
\(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\), \(0<z<1\), equivalently \(p=s=z\).  Put
\[
\theta=-\log z.
\]
Then the diagonal Palm gap law is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-gap-mass-computable-main}
g_k=\PP(G=k)
=z^k\{(1-z)(1+\theta k)-\theta z\},\qquad k\ge0 .
\end{equation}
In particular
\[
g_0=(1-z)-\theta z,\qquad
g_1=z\{(1-z)(1+\theta)-\theta z\}.
\]
Define
\[
\mu=\frac1{1-z}+\frac{\theta z}{(1-z)^2},
\qquad
\nu=\frac{1+z}{(1-z)^2}
     +\frac{\theta z(3+z)}{(1-z)^3},
\qquad
m_2=g_1+g_0^2 .
\]
Then the three inclusion coordinates at this diagonal point are
\[
\Rinc(z)=\frac1\mu(1,g_0,m_2)^\top ,
\]
and the quotient denominator is
\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}(z)=r_1(z)-r_0(z)^2
=-\frac{\theta^2z^2(1-z)^2}{(1-z+\theta z)^2}<0 .
\]
For the cycle reward variables
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-cycle-reward-y-main}
U_j=\left(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\right)^\top,
\qquad
L_j=G_j+1,\qquad
Y_j=U_j-\Rinc(z)L_j ,
\end{equation}
the \(Y_j\) sequence is one-dependent, centered, and has covariance
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-sigma-r-y-main}
\Sigma_r(z)=\frac1{\mu(z)}
\left[\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\right].
\end{equation}
Every entry in \eqref{eq:diagonal-sigma-r-y-main} is an absolutely convergent
finite or double sum under the explicit masses \eqref{eq:diagonal-gap-mass-computable-main};
indeed the indicators in \(U_j\) restrict the nontrivial terms to
\(G_j\in\{0,1\}\), while the factors containing \(L_j\) are sums of
polynomials in \(k\) against \(g_k=O(kz^k)\).
Let
\[
A_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
1&g_0&m_2\\
g_0&g_0&g_0^2\\
m_2&g_0^2&m_2
\end{pmatrix},
\quad
e_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
\mu\\ g_0\\ 2g_1+g_0^2
\end{pmatrix},
\quad
d_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
\mu\\ \mu g_0\\ \mu g_1+g_0^2
\end{pmatrix},
\]
and
\[
B_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
1&g_0&m_2\\
g_0&g_0^2&g_0m_2\\
m_2&g_0(g_1+g_0)&g_1^2+g_1g_0^2+g_0^3
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
With \(R=\Rinc(z)\), the complete-cycle inclusion covariance on the exchange
diagonal is the finite expression
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}
\Sigma_r(z)=\frac1\mu\left\{
A_z-e_zR^\top-Re_z^\top+\nu RR^\top
+B_z-d_zR^\top+B_z^\top-Rd_z^\top
\right\}.
\end{equation}
Consequently the actual diagonal covariance used by the projected-Gaussian
statistic is the reproducible \(2\times2\) matrix
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}
\Sigma_\sigma(z)=D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\,\Sigma_r(z)\,D\Phi(\Rinc(z))^\top
\end{equation}
where \(D\Phi\) is the explicit Jacobian in
\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}, evaluated at
the displayed \(\Rinc(z)\).  No infinite covariance-tail truncation or simulation
input is hidden in \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}: for a fixed
\(z\), all entries are obtained by ordinary arithmetic from
\((z,\theta,g_0,g_1)\), equivalently from the absolutely convergent
finite/double sums in \eqref{eq:diagonal-sigma-r-y-main}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the equal-rate diagonal the gap law is the one in
\eqref{eq:equal-rate-dictionary-main}, which gives
\eqref{eq:diagonal-gap-mass-computable-main}.  Hence
\[
\EE(G+1)=\sum_{k\ge0}\PP(G\ge k)=\mu,\qquad
\EE(G+1)^2=\sum_{k\ge0}(2k+1)\PP(G\ge k)=\nu ,
\]
which gives the displayed closed forms by summing the two elementary geometric
series and their first two derivatives.

For the inclusion-cycle reward in
\Cref{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main},
\[
U_j=\left(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\right)^\top .
\]
Since the gaps are i.i.d., \(\EE U_j=(1,g_0,g_1+g_0^2)^\top\), proving
\(\Rinc(z)=\EE U_j/\mu\).  The quotient denominator formula follows by direct
substitution and simplification; it is strictly negative because
\(0<z<1\) and \(\theta>0\).

The matrix \(A_z\) is \(\EE(U_0U_0^\top)\), \(e_z=\EE((G_0+1)U_0)\), and
\(B_z=\EE(U_0U_1^\top)\).  The vector \(d_z=\EE(U_0(G_1+1))\); the only
non-factorized entry is the third component, where
\[
\EE\{(\mathbf1_{\{G_0=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_0=0,G_1=0\}})(G_1+1)\}
=\mu g_1+g_0^2 .
\]
Writing \(Y_j=U_j-\Rinc(z)(G_j+1)\), the reward sequence is one-dependent and
\[
\Sigma_r(z)
=\frac1\mu\{\EE(Y_0Y_0^\top)+\EE(Y_0Y_1^\top)+\EE(Y_1Y_0^\top)\}.
\]
Expanding the three expectations gives exactly
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}.  The last display is then the
delta-method sandwich for the symmetric quotient coordinates
\(\Phi(\Rinc(z))=(p+s,ps)\), whose Jacobian was computed in
\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}.
\end{proof}

The main theorem uses
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main} only through the printed
branch certificate in
\Cref{app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.  The complete
interval transcript and the outward-rounded verifier are deliberately outside
the mathematical flow of this section; see
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main,lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main,rem:worked-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.

\paragraph{Model verification.}
We now specialize the generic renewal tools to the standing record experiment
of \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}.  The exponential-tail lemma
verifies the renewal hypothesis for this model.  Theorem~C then states the
exact finite-record statistic for the three inclusion coordinates and applies
the generic complete-cycle CLT to that statistic.

\begin{lemma}[Killed-leakage gap exponential moment]\label{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main}
Under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, set
\[
r=\max\{p,s\}<1 .
\]
If \(p\ne s\), then there is a finite constant \(C\), depending only on the
fixed constrained D-MAP setting, such that
\[
g_k\le Cr^k\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
If \(p=s=r\), then there is a finite constant \(C\), depending only on the
fixed constrained D-MAP setting, such that
\[
g_k\le C(1+k)r^k\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
Equivalently, in all cases, for every \(\bar r\in(r,1)\) there is a finite
constant \(C_{\bar r}\) such that
\[
g_k\le C_{\bar r}\bar r^k\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
Consequently, for every
\[
0<\eta<-\log r,
\]
the gap and cycle length satisfy
\[
\EE e^{\eta G}<\infty,\qquad
\EE e^{\eta (G+1)}<\infty .
\]
Thus the regenerative exponential-moment hypothesis used below is a verified
consequence of the killed-leakage model, not an additional assumption.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
When \(p\ne s\), the displayed unequal-rate formula
\eqref{eq:gap-closed-main} gives
\[
|g_k|
\le
\left|\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(1-p)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}\right|p^k
+
\left|\frac{\Gamma(1-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}\right|s^k
\le C_1(p^k+s^k)
\le 2C_1r^k .
\]
Since \(g_k\ge0\), this is the stated unequal-rate bound.

On the equal-rate diagonal \(p=s=r=e^{-\Gamma\Delta\tau}\), write
\(y=\Gamma\Delta\tau\).  The equal-rate dictionary
\eqref{eq:equal-rate-dictionary-main} gives
\[
g_k=r^k\bigl((1-r)(1+yk)-yr\bigr).
\]
Hence \(|g_k|\le C_2(1+k)r^k\), proving the stated equal-rate bound.

The slack-rate formulation follows from these two bounds.  In the unequal-rate
case \(r^k\le\bar r^k\).  In the equal-rate case, with
\(q=r/\bar r<1\),
\[
(1+k)r^k=(1+k)q^k\bar r^k
\le \left(\sup_{m\ge0}(1+m)q^m\right)\bar r^k,
\]
and the supremum is finite.  Absorbing the displayed finite factors into
\(C_{\bar r}\) gives the claimed envelope.

Finally fix \(0<\eta<-\log r\).  In the equal-rate case,
\[
\EE e^{\eta G}
=\sum_{k\ge0}e^{\eta k}g_k
\le C_2\sum_{k\ge0}(1+k)(re^\eta)^k
\]
is finite because \(re^\eta<1\).  In the unequal-rate case the same argument
uses \(g_k\le Cr^k\) and the geometric series
\(\sum_{k\ge0}(re^\eta)^k\).  Finally,
\(\EE e^{\eta(G+1)}=e^\eta\EE e^{\eta G}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Stationary killed-leakage three-inclusion complete-cycle CLT]\label{cor:three-inclusion-complete-cycle-clt-main}
In the stationary version of \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, let
\(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) be the exact finite-record statistic from
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}:
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}U_j^{(m)},\qquad m=0,1,2,
\]
with empty sums equal to \(0\).  Put
\[
\Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top,\qquad
r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1),\qquad
\mu=\EE(G+1).
\]
In Palm cycle coordinates,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:inclusion-cycle-reward-identities-main}
U_j^{(0)}=1,\qquad
U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},\qquad
U_j^{(2)}
=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,\ G_{j+1}=0\}},
\end{equation}
and the centered reward is
\[
Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-r_0L_j,\,
U_j^{(1)}-r_1L_j,\,
U_j^{(2)}-r_2L_j\bigr)^\top .
\]
Then \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\), \(\EE Y_j=0\), and the reward sequence is
\(1\)-dependent.  With
\[
\mathcal T_1^{(r)}
=\Var(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma_r=\frac{\mathcal T_1^{(r)}}{\mu},
\]
one has
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r).
\]
The proof uses the \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\) endpoint replacement and the
renewal random-index replacement from
\Cref{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main}; the covariance
scale is exactly \(\mathcal T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The half-open cycle \([\tau_j,\tau_{j+1})\) contains only the click at
\(\tau_j\), so \(U_j^{(0)}=1\).  A one-step look-ahead is a click exactly
when the next click occurs one sampled step later, so
\(U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}}\).  For lag two, the contributing time must
again be the click at \(\tau_j\); the click two steps later occurs either
when \(G_j=1\), or when \(G_j=0\) and the following gap also has length zero.
This gives the displayed reward identities.

\Cref{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main}, applied to
\(Z_n^{(m)}=A_nA_{n+m}\), gives the Palm inversion identity
\[
\EE U_j^{(m)}=r_m\EE L_j,\qquad m=0,1,2.
\]
Thus \(\EE Y_j=0\).  The displayed identities show that \(Y_j\) is a
function only of \((G_j,G_{j+1})\), hence \(1\)-dependent, and
\[
\|Y_j\|\le C(1+G_j)
\]
for a fixed finite \(C\).  The exponential moment required by the generic
proposition is supplied by
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main}.

For each coordinate,
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r_m
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}\{U_j^{(m)}-r_mL_j\}
+\frac{r_m}{N}\left\{\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}L_j-N\right\}.
\]
The complete cycles used by \(J_{m,N}\) differ from the calendar window
\([0,N)\) by only the initial incomplete cycle, the terminal incomplete
cycle, and a fixed look-ahead cycle.  Their total length is
\(O_{\PP}(\log N)\), by the endpoint proof in
\Cref{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main}.  Therefore the
second displayed term is \(o_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), and the first term has the
same \(N^{1/2}\)-limit as \(N^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j\).
\Cref{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main} then supplies the
deterministic \(1\)-dependent CLT, the endpoint replacement, and the
random-index replacement \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\).  The resulting calendar-time
covariance is \(\mathcal T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[CLT reduction for inclusion coordinates]\label{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main}
Under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, let
\(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\), \(K_N\), \(L_j=G_j+1\), \(U_j^{(m)}\),
\(\Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top\), and \(\mu=\EE L_0\) be as in
\Cref{cor:three-inclusion-complete-cycle-clt-main}.  Define
\[
Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-r_0L_j,\,
U_j^{(1)}-r_1L_j,\,
U_j^{(2)}-r_2L_j\bigr)^\top
=f(G_j,G_{j+1}),
\]
where
\[
U_j^{(0)}=1,\qquad
U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},\qquad
U_j^{(2)}
=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}} .
\]
Then
\[
\EE Y_j=0,\qquad (Y_j)_{j\in\mathbb Z}\ \text{is \(1\)-dependent},
\qquad
\frac{K_N}{N}\xrightarrow{\PP}\frac1\mu,
\]
and the finite-record statistic has the cycle-sum reduction
\begin{equation}\label{eq:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main}
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)
=
\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_{\PP}(1).
\end{equation}
Consequently, with
\[
T_1^{(r)}
=\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
 +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma_r=\frac{T_1^{(r)}}{\mu},
\]
one has
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r).
\]
The same reduction and limit hold for a finite hidden start after a
post-burn-in block of length \(L_N\to\infty\), with \(N\) interpreted as the
retained block length and with all cycle indices formed from that retained
word.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Palm reward identities are exactly
\eqref{eq:inclusion-cycle-reward-identities-main}.  Palm inversion for the
local windows gives \(\EE U_j^{(m)}=r_m\EE L_j\), hence \(\EE Y_j=0\).  The
displayed formulas make \(Y_j\) a function of the two adjacent gaps
\((G_j,G_{j+1})\), so independent renewal gaps imply \(1\)-dependence.

It remains to isolate the calendar-window error.  For each coordinate,
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r_m
=
\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}\{U_j^{(m)}-r_mL_j\}
+\frac{r_m}{N}\left\{\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}L_j-N\right\}.
\]
The complete cycles entering \(J_{m,N}\) differ from the prefix
\(\{0,\ldots,K_N-1\}\) only by the initial incomplete cycle, the terminal
incomplete cycle, and the fixed look-ahead cycles needed by the local
windows.  By the endpoint estimate in
\Cref{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main} and the exponential
gap moment of \Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main}, the total
omitted length is \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\).  Therefore both the length correction
and the replacement of \(J_{m,N}\) by the common cycle prefix are
\(o_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), which proves
\eqref{eq:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main}.

The renewal law of large numbers gives \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\).  Applying the
fixed-\(1\)-dependent multivariate CLT to the deterministic cycle prefix and
then the random-index replacement in
\Cref{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main} gives the displayed
Gaussian limit with covariance \(T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).

For a finite hidden start, apply
\Cref{lem:finite-hidden-start-path-contraction-main} with \(L=L_N\).  The
retained cycle-index estimator, the retained complete-cycle count, and the
retained cycle-sum approximation are measurable functions of the retained
visible \(N\)-word.  Hence their joint law under the finite-start protocol
differs from the stationary-start retained-word law by at most
\(C\theta^{L_N}\to0\) in total variation, uniformly in \(N\).  The stationary
\(o_{\PP}(1)\) reduction, the convergence \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\), and the Gaussian
limit therefore transfer to the finite-start retained block.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma E-local: explicit local projection at the exchange diagonal]\label{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main}
Let
\[
F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\},\qquad
\sigma_z=(2z,z^2),\quad
H_z=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le zh_1\},\quad 0<z<1.
\]
There exists \(\varepsilon_z>0\) such that \(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) is single-valued
on \(B_{\varepsilon_z}(\sigma_z)\).  Moreover, for every \(M<\infty\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exchange-diagonal-local-projection-rate-main}
\sup_{\|h\|\le M}\left\|
N^{1/2}\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N)-\sigma_z\}
-\Pi_{H_z}h\right\|\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
In fact the sharper deterministic bound
\begin{equation}\label{eq:explicit-local-projection-expansion-main}
