\sup_{\|h\|\le M}\left\|
N^{1/2}\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N)-\sigma_z\}
-\Pi_{H_z}h\right\|=O_M(N^{-1/2})
\end{equation}
holds, where the implicit constant may depend on \(z\) and \(M\).
Consequently, if
\[
H_N:=\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_z)\Rightarrow Z
\qquad\text{and}\qquad H_N=O_{\PP}(1),
\]
then, with
\(\widehat\sigma_N=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw})\),
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_z)
=\Pi_{H_z}H_N+o_{\PP}(1)
\Rightarrow \Pi_{H_z}Z .
\]
Equivalently, the local projection is Hadamard directionally differentiable at
\(\sigma_z\), tangentially to \(\mathbb R^2\), with derivative
\(h\mapsto\Pi_{H_z}h\).  No global nonexpansiveness of the nonconvex
projection selection is used.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{lem:feasible-set-computable-projection-main}, \(F\) is the compact
hypograph
\[
F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):0\le\sigma_1\le2,
\ell_F(\sigma_1)\le\sigma_2\le\sigma_1^2/4\}.
\]
Choose \(\delta>0\) such that \(2z-2\delta>0\), \(2z+2\delta<2\), and
\[
z^2-\ell_F(2z)>4\delta .
\]
Continuity of \(\ell_F\) then gives, after reducing \(\delta\) if necessary,
\[
F\cap\{|s-2z|<2\delta,\ |y-z^2|<2\delta\}
=\{(s,y): |s-2z|<2\delta,\ |y-z^2|<2\delta,\ y\le s^2/4\}.
\]
The lower box-boundary piece is therefore absent from this neighbourhood, and
only the exchange parabola \(y=s^2/4\) can be active.  Hence, in this local
neighbourhood of \(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\), the feasible set is exactly the
hypograph
\[
F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):\sigma_2\le\sigma_1^2/4\}.
\]

We first prove the scaled projection expansion in this local hypograph.
Write \(e=N^{-1/2}\), \(h=(a,b)\), and introduce the scaled boundary
\[
\gamma_e(t)=\left(t,\ zt+\frac{e t^2}{4}\right).
\]
Indeed,
\[
\sigma_z+e\,\gamma_e(t)
=\left(2z+et,\ z^2+ezt+\frac{e^2t^2}{4}\right)
=\left(2z+et,\ \frac{(2z+et)^2}{4}\right).
\]
Thus the local scaled feasible set is
\[
K_e=\left\{u\in\mathbb R^2:
u_2\le zu_1+\frac{e u_1^2}{4}\right\}.
\]
Equivalently, for \(\sigma=\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N\), local feasibility is
\[
h_2\le zh_1+\frac{h_1^2}{4\sqrt N}.
\]
The limiting half-space projection is explicit.  With
\[
A=1+z^2,\qquad d=b-za,\qquad n_z=(-z,1)^\top ,
\]
\[
\Pi_{H_z}h=
\begin{cases}
h,&d\le0,\\[2mm]
h-\dfrac{d}{A}n_z
=\left(\dfrac{a+zb}{A},\ z\dfrac{a+zb}{A}\right),&d>0.
\end{cases}
\]

Fix \(M<\infty\).  If \(\|h\|\le M\) and \(d\le0\), then \(h\in K_e\) because
\(e a^2/4\ge0\), and the local projection is exactly \(h=\Pi_{H_z}h\).  If
\(0<d\le e a^2/4\), then \(h\in K_e\) but \(h\notin H_z\), so the local
projection is \(h\), while
\[
\|h-\Pi_{H_z}h\|=\frac{d}{\sqrt A}\le \frac{eM^2}{4\sqrt A}.
\]
It remains to consider \(d>e a^2/4\).  Then \(h\) lies strictly above the
local parabola.  For a fixed first coordinate \(t\), the closest point of
\(K_e\) to \(h\) has second coordinate \(zt+et^2/4\); hence the local
projection is \(\gamma_e(t_e)\), where \(t_e\) minimizes
\[
q_e(t)=(t-a)^2+\left(zt+\frac{e t^2}{4}-b\right)^2 .
\]
The minimizer is uniformly bounded on the \(M\)-window: if
\[
R_M:=2M+\frac{M^2}{4},\qquad C_M:=M+R_M+1,
\]
then, for \(e\le1\),
\[
q_e(a)=\left(za+\frac{e a^2}{4}-b\right)^2\le R_M^2 .
\]
If \(|t|>C_M\), then \(|t-a|>R_M\), so \(q_e(t)>R_M^2\).  Thus every
minimizer lies in \([-C_M,C_M]\) for all sufficiently small \(e\).  On this
interval,
\[
\frac12q_e''(t)
=1+\left(z+\frac{et}{2}\right)^2
+\frac e2\left(zt+\frac{e t^2}{4}-b\right)
\ge \frac A2
\]
for all sufficiently small \(e\), uniformly in \(\|h\|\le M\).  Hence the
minimizer is unique.  Its normal equation is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:parabola-normal-equation-main}
0=\frac12q_e'(t_e)
=(t_e-a)+\left(z+\frac{e t_e}{2}\right)
\left(zt_e+\frac{e t_e^2}{4}-b\right).
\end{equation}
Equivalently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:parabola-normal-expanded-main}
0=A t_e-(a+zb)
+e\left(\frac{3z}{4}t_e^2-\frac b2t_e\right)
+\frac{e^2}{8}t_e^3 .
\end{equation}
Put
\[
t_0=\frac{a+zb}{A},\qquad
R(t)=\frac{3z}{4}t^2-\frac b2t .
\]
Subtracting \(A t_0-(a+zb)=0\) from
\eqref{eq:parabola-normal-expanded-main} gives
\[
A(t_e-t_0)=-eR(t_e)-\frac{e^2}{8}t_e^3 .
\]
Since \(|t_e|\le C_M\) and \(|b|\le M\), this identity gives the uniform
normal-equation solution
\[
t_e=t_0+O_M(e).
\]
Writing the projected boundary displacement as
\[
k=\gamma_e(t_e)
=\left(t_e,\ zt_e+\frac{e t_e^2}{4}\right),
\]
and using \(d=b-za\), this is equivalently the first-order normal-projection
formula
\[
k
=h-\frac{h_2-zh_1}{1+z^2}(-z,1)+O_M(e),
\]
uniformly for \(\|h\|\le M\) in the infeasible case.  The feasible cases above
give the same display after replacing \(h_2-zh_1\) by its positive part, up to
the \(O_M(e)\) boundary perturbation.
Substituting into the boundary parametrization yields
\[
\gamma_e(t_e)
=\left(t_0,\ zt_0\right)+O_M(e)
=\Pi_{H_z}h+O_M(e),
\]
uniformly over all \(\|h\|\le M\) in the case \(d>ea^2/4\).  Combining the
three cases,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scaled-local-hypograph-projection-main}
\sup_{\|h\|\le M}
\left\|\Pi_{K_e}h-\Pi_{H_z}h\right\|=O_M(e).
\end{equation}

It remains only to identify the local hypograph projection with the
tie-broken projection onto the full feasible set.  The same second-derivative
calculation at \(e=1\), after translating the first coordinate by \(2z\) and
shrinking the rectangle if necessary, gives a unique local nearest point for
each point in a sufficiently small ball about \(\sigma_z\).  The preceding
scaled strict-convexity argument gives the corresponding \(N\)-window
uniqueness uniformly for \(\|h\|\le M\) in the rectangle
\(|s-2z|<2\delta\), \(|y-z^2|<2\delta\), while every point of
\(F\) outside that rectangle has positive distance from \(\sigma_z\).  Taking
\(\varepsilon_z>0\) smaller than this separation margin and then taking
\(N\) large enough for \(\|h\|\le M\) to imply
\(\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N\in B_{\varepsilon_z}(\sigma_z)\), no remote point of
\(F\) can tie the local minimizer.  Hence
\[
\sqrt N\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N)-\sigma_z\}
=\Pi_{K_{N^{-1/2}}}h
\]
uniformly for \(\|h\|\le M\) and all sufficiently large \(N\).  With
\eqref{eq:scaled-local-hypograph-projection-main}, this is exactly
\eqref{eq:exchange-diagonal-local-projection-rate-main}, with the sharper
\(O_M(N^{-1/2})\) rate.

Finally, tightness gives: for every \(\eta>0\) choose \(M\) with
\(\PP(\|H_N\|>M)<\eta\) for all large \(N\).  On \(\{\|H_N\|\le M\}\), the
uniform display gives
\[
\sqrt N\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\sigma_z+H_N/\sqrt N)-\sigma_z\}
=\Pi_{H_z}H_N+o_{\PP}(1).
\]
The continuous mapping theorem for the continuous half-space projection then
gives \(\Pi_{H_z}H_N\Rightarrow\Pi_{H_z}Z\).
\end{proof}

\begin{assumption}[Finite-record master assumption]\label{ass:finite-record-master-main}
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main,thm:finite-record-quotient-delta-main,thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}
are read under \Cref{subsec:finite-record-report-scope-main} and the following named
hypotheses.  These hypotheses collect the finite-record law, estimand,
reporting gate, covariance-estimation, projection, and diagonal calibration
rules; the theorem statements below invoke these names instead of repeating
the full scope contract.
The algorithmic recipes implementing these conventions are collected after
Theorem~E so that the assumption block is the single pre-theorem contract.
No item below is a data-driven model-selection rule.  When a theorem displays
coverage or confidence language, that language is read only under
\Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main} and only for the fixed statistic,
branch, thresholds, and reporting gate named before the record is used.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{FR-\arabic*.},ref=FR-\arabic*,leftmargin=3.0em,itemsep=0.45em]
\item\label{fr:law-main}\textbf{Record law and retained word.}
Assumption~\ref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main} holds.  The observed
data are the retained post-burn-in visible word of length \(N\).  All
complete-cycle sets, batches, projections, Wald statistics, and finite-record
reports are functions only of that retained word.

\item\label{fr:estimands-main}\textbf{Visible-record estimands.}
The inclusion estimand is
\[
\Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2),\qquad r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1).
\]
On the stable quotient chart \(r_0>0\) and \(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\), the symmetric
quotient estimand is
\[
\sigma_{\rm sym}=\Phi(\Rinc)=(p+s,ps),
\qquad
\lambda=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}.
\]
The finite visible record identifies \(\{p,s\}\).  For known design interval
\(\Delta\tau\), this gives the unordered numerical rate set
\[
\left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\},
\]
and, on the exchange diagonal \(p=s=z\), the common numerical rate
\(-\log z/\Delta\tau\).  Without known \(\Delta\tau\), the corresponding
rate-scale estimands are only \(\{-\log p,-\log s\}\) and, on the diagonal,
\(-\log z\).  Ordered labelled rates are not visible-record estimands here.
The retained-record coverage statements in Theorems~C--E therefore concern
\(\Rinc\), \(\Phi(\Rinc)\), unordered roots, known-interval unordered rates or
dimensionless products as applicable, the lag-two visible covariance, and the
exchange-diagonal common-root/common-rate event.  A confidence statement for
the ordered vector \((\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r})\) is outside the quotient-only
visible-law inverse and appears only after the separate side-data assumptions
of \Cref{cor:side-data-labelled-rate-wald-main}.

\item\label{fr:protocol-main}\textbf{Operational gate and deterministic thresholds.}
The deterministic thresholds are
\begin{equation}\label{prot:finite-record-main}
\begin{aligned}
b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor,\qquad
t_N=N^{-1/4},\qquad
\varepsilon_N=N^{-1/4},\qquad
\eta_N=N^{-1/8},\\
\delta_N^{\rm root}=N^{-1/8},
\qquad
\delta_N^{\rm MC}=N^{-2}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
and the rank-one diagonal angle threshold used in the reporting algorithm is
\(a_N=N^{-1/8}\).  Fix once and for all an integer \(K_{\min}\ge2\).  With
\(K_N=\max\{k:\tau_k<N\}\), with \(K_N=-1\) if no click is observed, with
\(M_N\) the separated-batch count in Algorithm~1, and with
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}=
\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^2,
\]
the reporting event is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-record-reporting-event-main}
\mathcal E_N=
\{K_N\ge K_{\min},\ M_N\ge2,\ \widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\ge\varepsilon_N,
|\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}|\ge\varepsilon_N\}.
\end{equation}
On \(\mathcal E_N\) the pre-specified fixed-model protocol evaluates the
estimator, sandwich covariance, retained rank, and any finite-record event
specified by the applicable theorem branch.  On \(\mathcal E_N^c\) it reports
insufficient complete cycles and forms no finite-record report.  The event is
a finite-record reporting gate, not a diagnostic pass/fail certificate and not
a population-level exclusion; its high-probability validity on the stable
quotient chart is proved separately in
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.  The separated-batch covariance
rate is \(N^{-1/3}\), the retained-rank threshold is larger than the
covariance-estimation error, \(N^{1/2}(\delta_N^{\rm root})^2\to\infty\), and
\(t_N/a_N\to0\).  The root threshold \(\delta_N^{\rm root}\) is deterministic
chart-reporting input; the separate value
\(\delta_N^{\rm MC}=N^{-2}\) is used only as the DKW Monte Carlo error
probability in simulation certificates.  The two thresholds are never
identified.

\item\label{fr:covariance-main}\textbf{Covariance estimator and retained rank.}
Algorithm~1 is the covariance rule.  On \(\mathcal E_N\) it reports
\(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\); on \(\mathcal E_N^c\) it withholds the covariance
matrix and no finite-record report is formed.  Probability-limit statements use only
the auxiliary total-space completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3},
\]
which is not an operational replacement.  The quotient covariance used on the
reported event is the sandwich
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
=D\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})
 \widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
 D\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})^\top,
\]
after the same feasible projection and chart retention as Algorithm~2.
For full-space probability limits its auxiliary completion is
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{2\times2}.
\]
Diagonal statistics use exactly the empirical eigenvalues of
\(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\) retained above \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\).  The consistency
needed for that retained-rank step is not assumed here: it is supplied for
the inclusion covariance by \Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main} and, on
the exchange diagonal, pushed through the quotient Jacobian in
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}.

\item\label{fr:projection-main}\textbf{Feasible projection and chart retention.}
Algorithm~2 is the projection and chart-retention rule.  The raw symmetric
coordinate is projected to
\[
\widetilde\sigma_N=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})),
\qquad
F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\}.
\]
At separated roots with \(\Delta_\sigma>0\), the theorem reports only the
unordered root and rate sets.  A labelled separated-rate report is withheld
unless an external branch map satisfies
\Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}.

\item\label{fr:diagonal-main}\textbf{Exchange-diagonal local projection.}
At \(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\), \(0<z<1\), the local-reach hypothesis is exactly
\Cref{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main}: with probability tending
to one, the global tie-broken projection agrees with the unique local
projection, and its tangent projection is onto
\[
H_z=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le zh_1\}.
\]

\item\label{fr:mc-main}\textbf{Diagonal calibration and conservative reporting.}
The exchange-diagonal statistic uses the \(t_N\)-thresholded pseudoinverse and
the ideal conditional projected-Gaussian lower quantile
\(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\).  The main text uses only the following
pre-specified certificate interface.  A diagonal theorem-level report is
available only for a candidate fixed in advance whose appendices supply both a
population branch certificate for the computable covariance
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) from \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main} and a
critical-value certificate for the same retained branch.  For the submitted
manuscript this occurs only at \(z_0=1/2\).  If either certificate is absent or
inconclusive, the projected-Gaussian output is a diagnostic display only and
is not a finite-record confidence report.  Such diagnostic output neither
selects a valid branch nor certifies the model.  The full interval-arithmetic
transcripts, mechanical bisection rules, and finite accept/reject/undecided
report formats are not part of this assumption block; they are the certificate
protocols in
\Cref{def:finite-diagonal-report-main,prop:finite-diagonal-report-master-main,app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{assumption}

\begin{lemma}[Finite-record reporting gate is asymptotically open]\label{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main},
\ref{fr:law-main}, \ref{fr:estimands-main}, and \ref{fr:protocol-main}.
At any positive constrained D-MAP population point of
\Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, write
\[
r_0=\rho,\qquad r_1=\rho a,\qquad \Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}:=r_1-r_0^2=\rho(a-\rho).
\]
Then \(r_0=\rho>0\) and \(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=\rho(a-\rho)<0\).
With
\[
b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor,\qquad
\varepsilon_N=N^{-1/4},
\]
and fixed \(K_{\min}\ge2\), the reporting event \(\mathcal E_N\) in
\eqref{eq:finite-record-reporting-event-main},
\[
\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0 .
\]
More explicitly,
\[
\PP(K_N<K_{\min})\to0,\qquad
\PP(M_N<2)\to0,
\]
and
\[
\PP(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}<\varepsilon_N)\to0,\qquad
\PP(|\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}|<\varepsilon_N)\to0 .
\]
The same conclusions hold in the finite hidden-start experiment after any
deterministic burn-in \(L_N\to\infty\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The D-MAP model identities are those of
\Cref{lem:stationary-main,thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality}:
\(r_0=\rho\), \(r_1=\rho a\), and
\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=r_1-r_0^2=\rho(a-\rho).
\]
At a positive constrained D-MAP point, \(\rho>0\) and
\(a-\rho<0\), so \(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}<0\).  Thus the denominator test below is a consequence
of the population law, not an additional finite-record assumption.

The fixed low-cycle assertion is exactly
\Cref{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main}, with \(C=K_{\min}\).  Since
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\) and \(d_N=b_N+1=o(N)\), the all-complete-cycle
separated-batch count satisfies \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one by
\Cref{prop:calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin-main}; equivalently, the
renewal law gives
\[
K_N/N\to\rho=1/\mu>0
\]
in probability, so \(K_N\ge K_{\min}\) with probability tending to one, and
the retained separated-batch count satisfies
\[
M_N\sim K_N/(b_N+1)
\]
up to fixed endpoint and look-ahead deletions.  With
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), this implies \(M_N\to\infty\) in probability,
hence \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one.

\Cref{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main} gives
\(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
\(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\to r_0\) in probability.  Because
\(r_0>0\) and \(\varepsilon_N=N^{-1/4}\to0\),
\[
\PP(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}<\varepsilon_N)
\le
\PP(|\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r_0|>r_0/2)
\]
for all sufficiently large \(N\), and the right side tends to zero.

The map \(x\mapsto x_1-x_0^2\) is continuous, so
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}
=\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
 -(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^2
\xrightarrow{\PP}
r_1-r_0^2=\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=\rho(a-\rho)<0 .
\]
Since \(|\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}|>0\) and \(\varepsilon_N\to0\),
\[
\PP(|\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}|<\varepsilon_N)
\le
\PP(|\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}-\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}|>|\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}|/2)
\]
eventually, and this probability tends to zero.  A union bound over the four
complementary gate conditions proves \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\).

For a finite hidden start, \(\mathcal E_N\), \(K_N\), \(M_N\),
\(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\), and \(\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}\) are measurable functions
of the retained \(N\)-word.  The total-variation transfer in
\Cref{lem:finite-hidden-start-path-contraction-main} therefore carries each
stationary-start probability limit to the post-burn-in retained block.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[No-post-selection reporting event for finite-record reports]\label{conv:boxed-retained-coverage-main}
Let \(\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux}\) be a fixed auxiliary acceptance event defined
before the retained record is inspected, and let
\[
\mathcal A_N^{\rm rep}:=\mathcal E_N\cap\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux}.
\]
If \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\), then
\[
\left|\PP(\mathcal A_N^{\rm rep})-\PP(\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux})\right|
\le \PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0 .
\]
This proposition fails to justify any event \(\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux}\) chosen
after inspecting diagnostics, reporting branches, retained ranks, covariance
estimates, candidate diagonal points, or critical-value transcripts.  Thus each
detailed finite-record assertion below concerns the reported event
\(\mathcal E_N\cap\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux}\) under the fixed population law,
model, estimand, statistic, deterministic thresholds, branch, covariance rule,
and reporting gate specified before inspection.  On \(\mathcal E_N^c\) the
report is withheld.  Auxiliary zero completions used in proofs are not
reported confidence sets and are never operational substitutes for the
withheld report.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(\mathcal A_N^{\rm rep}\subset\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux}\),
\[
0\le \PP(\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux})-\PP(\mathcal A_N^{\rm rep})
=\PP(\mathcal A_N^{\rm aux}\cap\mathcal E_N^c)
\le \PP(\mathcal E_N^c).
\]
The convergence follows from the assumed vanishing withholding probability.
The fixed-event hypothesis is essential: the displayed inequality is not a
post-selection theorem for an auxiliary event chosen after seeing the record.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Retained-cycle CLT reduction for adjacent-gap rewards]\label{lem:retained-cycle-covariance-adjacent-gap-main}
Work under the retained stationary renewal representation in
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, with i.i.d. gaps \(G_j\), cycle lengths
\(L_j=G_j+1\), \(\mu=\EE L_0\in(0,\infty)\), and an exponential gap moment.
For the three inclusion coordinates put
\[
U_j=
\bigl(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\bigr)^\top,
\qquad
Y_j=U_j-\Rinc L_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1}),
\]
where \(\Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top\) and
\(r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1)\).  Then \(\EE Y_j=0\),
\(\EE\|Y_j\|^4<\infty\), and \((Y_j)\) is \(1\)-dependent.  Define
\[
T_1^{(r)}
=\Var(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma_r=T_1^{(r)}/\mu .
\]
Let
\[
S_k=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}L_j,\qquad
K_N=\max\{k:S_k<N\},
\]
with the harmless auxiliary value \(K_N=0\) if no retained click is present.
Let \(J_N\) be the common complete-cycle, terminal-look-ahead-retained index
set used by \(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) and Algorithm~1.  Then the
endpoint and terminal-look-ahead deletion has the explicit negligible scale
\begin{equation}\label{eq:retained-cycle-endpoint-deletion-main}
\left\|
\sum_{j\in J_N}Y_j-\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j
\right\|=O_{\PP}(\log N),
\qquad
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)
=\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_{\PP}(1).
\end{equation}
Consequently
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r).
\]

The retained covariance estimator for this same adjacent-gap reward, including
the exact \(J_N\), \(M_N\), separator, multiplier \(K_N/N\), empirical batch
mean, and plug-in centering by \(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\), is the object
in \Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main}.  In
particular, for the paper's protocol \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), its
auxiliary total-space completion obeys
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3}).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The formula for \(U_j\) is the adjacent-gap version of the three inclusion
rewards: a Palm cycle contributes one left-end click, the lag-one coordinate
is \(\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}}\), and the lag-two coordinate is
\(\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\).  Palm inversion gives
\(\EE U_j=\Rinc\,\EE L_j\), hence \(\EE Y_j=0\).  Since \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\)
and the gaps are i.i.d., the sequence is \(1\)-dependent; the exponential gap
tail gives the fourth moment and the maximal-cycle bound
\(\max_{j\le K_N+2}L_j=O_{\PP}(\log N)\).  Deleting the fixed initial,
terminal, and terminal-look-ahead cycles can therefore change the retained
cycle sum by only \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\), proving the first estimate in
\eqref{eq:retained-cycle-endpoint-deletion-main}.  The second estimate follows
from the same endpoint bound after dividing by \(\sqrt N\), and the displayed
CLT is the ordinary \(1\)-dependent CLT for the cycle rewards combined with
the renewal random-index replacement \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\).

The covariance statement is exactly
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main} with
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Detailed form of Theorem~C: fixed-setting retained-record inclusion CLT]\label{prop:finite-record-inference-audit-detailed}
Assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, specifically
\ref{fr:law-main}, \ref{fr:estimands-main}, \ref{fr:protocol-main}, and
\ref{fr:covariance-main}.
All confidence-language consequences of this theorem are conditional on
\Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}, the fixed pre-specified reporting
gate \(\mathcal E_N\), and the convention in
\Cref{conv:boxed-retained-coverage-main}.
At positive constrained D-MAP population points, the reporting event
\(\mathcal E_N\) obeys \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\) by
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.
From the retained visible word form the complete-cycle inclusion estimator
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}U_j^{(m)},\qquad m=0,1,2,
\]
with the empty-sum convention in
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}, and write
\(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
  \widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
  \widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^\top\).  Let
\[
\Rinc=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top,\qquad r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1).
\]
In Palm cycle coordinates put
\[
U_j=
\bigl(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\bigr)^\top,
\qquad
L_j=G_j+1,\qquad
Y_j=U_j-\Rinc L_j .
\]
Then
\[
\EE Y_j=0,\qquad (Y_j)\text{ is \(1\)-dependent},
\]
and
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)
=\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_{\PP}(1).
\]

Consequently
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r),
\]
where
\[
\Sigma_r=\frac1\mu\left\{\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)
+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\right\},
\qquad \mu=\EE L_0 .
\]
The auxiliary total-space separated-batch matrix
\(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\) from
\ref{fr:covariance-main} obeys
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3}).
\]
The covariance-rate assertion is exactly
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main} with the protocol
choice \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\),
\[
Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})=U_j-\Rinc L_j,\qquad
\Delta_N=\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}).
\]
That proposition is the single place where the exact \(J_N\), \(M_N\),
separator, \(K_N/N\) multiplier, empirical batch mean, and plug-in
perturbation are verified for Algorithm~1.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Pre-diagonal quotient delta method]\label{prop:finite-record-pre-diagonal-quotient-rule-main}
Let \(\Rinch_N\) be any three-coordinate finite-record estimator.  On the
population inverse domain
\[
r_0>0,\qquad \Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}(\Rinc):=r_1-r_0^2\ne0,
\]
define
\[
a(\Rinc)=\frac{r_1}{r_0},\qquad
u_2(\Rinc)=\frac{r_2}{r_0},\qquad
\lambda(\Rinc)=\frac{u_2(\Rinc)-r_0}{a(\Rinc)-r_0},
\]
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-inverse-delta-map-main}
\Phi(\Rinc)=
\bigl(1-a(\Rinc)+\lambda(\Rinc),\,
r_0(1-\lambda(\Rinc))-a(\Rinc)+\lambda(\Rinc)\bigr).
\end{equation}
Then \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on a neighbourhood of every point in this domain, and
its Jacobian is the one displayed in
\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}.  If
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N-\Rinc)\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r),
\]
then
\[
\sqrt N\{\Phi(\Rinch_N)-\Phi(\Rinc)\}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_{\sigma}),\qquad
\Sigma_{\sigma}=D\Phi(\Rinc)\Sigma_rD\Phi(\Rinc)^\top .
\]
Moreover, if a reported covariance estimator has an auxiliary total-space
completion \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\) satisfying
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\qquad t_N\downarrow0,\qquad N^{-1/2}=o(t_N),
\]
then the plug-in sandwich
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}
=\mathbf1_{\mathcal E_N}
D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}D\Phi(\Rinch_N)^\top
+\mathbf1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{2\times2}
\]
satisfies
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_{\sigma}\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N).
\]
If, in addition, the constrained D-MAP population has
\[
\Phi(\Rinc)=\sigma=(\sigma_1,\sigma_2)=(p+s,ps),
\qquad
\Delta_\sigma:=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2=(p-s)^2>0,
\]
then the two unordered roots of
\[
z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0
\]
are locally \(C^1\) functions of \(\sigma=\Phi(\Rinc)\).  The unordered
survival-factor set \(\{p,s\}\) is therefore identified locally from
\(\Phi(\Rinc)\).  For known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), the unordered
numerical rate set follows by applying
\[
z\longmapsto -\frac{\log z}{\Delta\tau}
\]
to the two roots.  Without known \(\Delta\tau\), the same roots identify only
the dimensionless products \(\{-\log p,-\log s\}
=\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\}\).  No diagonal
projection, exchange-diagonal confidence, or diagonal no-label conclusion is
asserted in this proposition.

\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The only possible poles of the displayed rational formula are \(r_0=0\) and
\(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}(\Rinc)=r_1-r_0^2=0\).  On the stated domain these poles are absent in a small
neighbourhood of \(\Rinc\), so \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\), with the already computed
derivative \eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}.  The
ordinary multivariate delta method gives the Gaussian limit.

The covariance statement is the same continuous-mapping argument at the
Jacobian level.  The CLT gives \(\Rinch_N-\Rinc=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Since
\(D\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on the retained neighbourhood,
\[
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)-D\Phi(\Rinc)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}),
\qquad
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(1).
\]
Submultiplicativity and the triangle inequality give
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left\|
D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}D\Phi(\Rinch_N)^\top
-D\Phi(\Rinc)\Sigma_rD\Phi(\Rinc)^\top
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\le
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}^2
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\quad+
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)-D\Phi(\Rinc)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\|\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\{\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}+\|D\Phi(\Rinc)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The first term is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\); the second is \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
\(o_{\PP}(t_N)\) by the stated threshold scale.

If \(\Delta_\sigma>0\), the discriminant is nonzero and the implicit-function
theorem applied to \(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2\) gives two local \(C^1\) root
charts; taking the set of their values gives the unordered root map, independent
of a branch labelling.  The logarithm map is \(C^1\) on compact subintervals of
\((0,1)\), so, for known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), the unordered
numerical rate set is the image of this unordered root set under
\(z\mapsto-\log z/\Delta\tau\).  Without known \(\Delta\tau\), the same root
set gives only the unordered dimensionless products.

\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Detailed form of Theorem~D: fixed-setting retained-record quotient delta method]\label{prop:finite-record-quotient-delta-detailed}
Assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, specifically
\ref{fr:law-main}--\ref{fr:projection-main}.  Assume also that the population
inverse is in the stable quotient chart
\[
r_0>0,\qquad \Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=r_1-r_0^2\ne0 .
\]
Define the quotient map
\[
\Phi(\Rinc)=\left(1-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda,\;
r_0(1-\lambda)-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda\right),
\qquad
\lambda=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}.
\]
Then
\[
\sqrt N\{\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})-\Phi(\Rinc)\}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma),
\qquad
\Sigma_\sigma=D\Phi(\Rinc)\Sigma_rD\Phi(\Rinc)^\top.
\]
For the constrained D-MAP population, \(\Phi(\Rinc)=(p+s,ps)\).  If
\(\Delta_\sigma=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2>0\), the polynomial
\(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2\) identifies the unordered survival-factor set
\(\{p,s\}\), and hence, for known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), the
unordered numerical rate set
\[
\left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\}.
\]
If \(\Delta\tau\) is not supplied as a known design interval, the
corresponding identified object is only the dimensionless unordered product set
\[
\{-\log p,-\log s\}
=\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Diagonal raw quotient CLT from the retained-record CLT and diagonal covariance formula]\label{lem:dmap-diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}
Assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, specifically
\ref{fr:law-main}--\ref{fr:projection-main}.  Fix a constrained D-MAP
exchange-diagonal population point \(p=s=z\), \(0<z<1\), put
\(\theta=-\log z\), and write \(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\).  Let \(\Rinc(z)\) be the
corresponding inclusion-coordinate vector in
\Cref{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main}.  Then the quotient denominator
has the explicit diagonal value
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-quotient-denominator-main}
\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}(z):=r_1(z)-r_0(z)^2
=-\frac{\theta^2z^2(1-z)^2}{(1-z+\theta z)^2}<0 .
\end{equation}
Consequently the diagonal point is not a singular point of the quotient map
\[
\Phi(\Rinc)=\left(1-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda,\;
r_0(1-\lambda)-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda\right),
\qquad
\lambda=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}.
\]
The map \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on a neighbourhood of \(\Rinc(z)\), and \(D\Phi\) is
locally bounded there.  Hence, if Theorem~C gives
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N-\Rinc(z))\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r(z)),
\]
then the raw diagonal symmetric-coordinate estimator
\[
\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}:=\Phi(\Rinch_N)
\]
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}
\sqrt N\{\Phi(\Rinch_N)-\Phi(\Rinc(z))\}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N\!\left(0,\,
D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\,\Sigma_r(z)\,D\Phi(\Rinc(z))^\top\right).
\end{equation}
For the constrained D-MAP population, \(\Phi(\Rinc(z))=\sigma_z\).

Moreover, for any reported inclusion covariance estimator whose auxiliary
total-space completion satisfies
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\qquad t_N\downarrow0,\qquad N^{-1/2}=o(t_N),
\]
the reported plug-in sandwich on \(\mathcal E_N\),
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
=D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}D\Phi(\Rinch_N)^\top,\qquad
\Sigma_\sigma(z)=D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\Sigma_r(z)D\Phi(\Rinc(z))^\top,
\]
has auxiliary total-space completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{2\times2}
\]
satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-sandwich-consistency-main}
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_\sigma(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N).
\end{equation}
In particular, with the separated-batch estimator from
\Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main} and \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\),
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_\sigma(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})=o_{\PP}(N^{-1/4}).
\]
Thus the raw diagonal CLT and the covariance consistency needed for the
exchange-diagonal projection theorem are consequences of Theorem~C,
\Cref{prop:finite-record-pre-diagonal-quotient-rule-main}, and the separated-batch
covariance theorem, not separate diagonal assumptions and not consequences of
Theorem~D or Theorem~E.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\Cref{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main} gives the diagonal coordinates
\(\Rinc(z)\).  Substituting the equal-rate dictionary with
\(\theta=-\log z\) into \(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=r_1-r_0^2\) gives
\eqref{eq:diagonal-quotient-denominator-main}.  Since \(0<z<1\),
\(\theta>0\), \(z>0\), \(1-z>0\), and
\[
1-z+\theta z>0,
\]
the denominator in \eqref{eq:diagonal-quotient-denominator-main} is nonzero
and \(D(z)<0\).  Also \(r_0(z)>0\).  Therefore the rational formula defining
\(\Phi\) has no pole on a small neighbourhood of \(\Rinc(z)\), and the derivative
displayed in \eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main} is
finite and locally bounded there.

Theorem~C,
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}, gives the displayed inclusion
CLT for the complete-cycle estimator \(\Rinch_N=\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\)
on the retained reporting branch.  The ordinary \(C^1\) delta method applied
to \(\Phi\), equivalently the diagonal specialization of the pre-diagonal
quotient rule \Cref{prop:finite-record-pre-diagonal-quotient-rule-main}, gives
\eqref{eq:diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}.  The population quotient-inverse
identity gives \(\Phi(\Rinc(z))=(p+s,ps)=(2z,z^2)=\sigma_z\).

It remains to check that the sandwich covariance consistency is inherited from
the inclusion-coordinate covariance estimator.  The CLT implies
\(\Rinch_N-\Rinc(z)=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
\(D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\to D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\) in probability; local boundedness gives
\[
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(1),\qquad
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)-D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(1).
\]
The regenerative covariance \(\Sigma_r(z)\) is a fixed finite matrix.  Using
the triangle inequality and submultiplicativity,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left\|
D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}D\Phi(\Rinch_N)^\top
-D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\Sigma_r(z)D\Phi(\Rinc(z))^\top
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\le
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}^2
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\quad+
\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)-D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\|\Sigma_r(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\{\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
+\|D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The first term is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\) by the assumed inclusion covariance rate.
For the second term, the concrete retained-rank choices in
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main} use \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\), and the displayed
hypothesis \(N^{-1/2}=o(t_N)\) is the only rate fact needed here.  The CLT gives
\(\|D\Phi(\Rinch_N)-D\Phi(\Rinc(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\)
because \(D\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on the retained neighbourhood.  Thus the second
term is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\), proving
\eqref{eq:diagonal-sandwich-consistency-main}.  With
\Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main} and
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), the inclusion covariance rate is
\(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})\), so the displayed \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})=o_{\PP}(N^{-1/4})\)
specialization follows.  The finite covariance formula is exactly
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main} on \(\mathcal E_N\).  On
\(\mathcal E_N^c\) the auxiliary zero completion is used only in the preceding
probability bound, while the operational protocol reports no covariance matrix.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local-projection expansion used in Theorem~E]\label{lem:local-projection-expansion-before-theorem-e-main}
For fixed \(z\in(0,1)\) and every \(M<\infty\), there is a constant
\(C_M<\infty\), depending only on \(z\) and \(M\), such that
\[
\sup_{\|h\|\le M}
\left\|
\sqrt N\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N)-\sigma_z\}
-\Pi_{H_z}h
\right\|
\le C_MN^{-1/2},
\]
where \(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\), \(H_z=\{h:h_2\le zh_1\}\), and
\(F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\}\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
This is the uniform expansion
\eqref{eq:explicit-local-projection-expansion-main}.  The argument is local:
near \(\sigma_z\), \(F\) is the hypograph
\[
F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):\sigma_2\le \sigma_1^2/4\}.
\]
For \(\sigma=\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N\), feasibility is
\[
h_2\le zh_1+\frac{h_1^2}{4\sqrt N}.
\]
If this holds, the local projection is \(\sigma\), and the only discrepancy
from \(\Pi_{H_z}h\) occurs in the \(O(N^{-1/2})\) boundary strip where
\(0<h_2-zh_1\le h_1^2/(4\sqrt N)\).  If feasibility fails, write the projected
boundary point as
\[
s_1=2z+\frac{k_1}{\sqrt N},\qquad
s_2=z^2+\frac{zk_1}{\sqrt N}+\frac{k_1^2}{4N}.
\]
The normal equation to the parabola gives, uniformly for \(\|h\|\le M\),
\[
k
=h-\frac{h_2-zh_1}{1+z^2}(-z,1)+O_M(N^{-1/2}).
\]
Taking the positive part across the feasible and infeasible split gives
\[
k=\Pi_{H_z}h+O_M(N^{-1/2}),
\]
which is the displayed bound.  \Cref{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main}
also verifies that the local projection is the tie-broken projection onto the
full compact set \(F\) in a sufficiently small neighbourhood of \(\sigma_z\).
\end{proof}

\recordscopebox{The diagonal inference result in this manuscript is an
ideal fixed-null asymptotic statement at \(z_0=1/2\).  It is not a simultaneous
confidence procedure over \(0<z<1\), not a data-dependent
model-selection-valid diagonal report, and not an executed finite-record
accept/reject report.  The article prints the \(z_0=1/2\) population branch
certificate, but it does not print a record-specific critical-value bracket
\([L,U]\), a statistic interval \([Q_N]\), or any complete finite diagonal
certificate tuple.}

\begin{proposition}[Detailed form of Theorem~E: ideal fixed-null exchange-diagonal asymptotics]\label{prop:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-detailed}
Fix
\[
z_0=\frac12,\qquad \sigma_0=(1,1/4),\qquad
H_{z_0}=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le z_0h_1\},
\]
and assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main},
\ref{fr:law-main}--\ref{fr:projection-main}.  Suppose the true constrained D-MAP
population satisfies \(\sigma=\sigma_0\), the printed interval certificate
\Cref{lem:z-half-diagonal-branch-certificate-main} proves
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=2,
\]
and
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_0)
\Rightarrow Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)).
\]
Let
\[
\widehat\sigma_N=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}),\qquad
X_N=\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_0),
\]
where \(F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\}\).  Then
\[
X_N\Rightarrow X:=\Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z .
\]
The full-rank certificate makes \(\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\) invertible.  Put
\[
T:=X^\top\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}X,
\]
and let \(c_{1-\alpha}\) be the lower \((1-\alpha)\)-quantile of \(T\).  With
\[
E_N:=\mathcal E_N
\]
the retained-record gate, for each fixed \(0<\alpha<1\),
\[
\PP_{\sigma_0}\{E_N\cap[
X_N^\top\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}X_N\le c_{1-\alpha}]\}
\longrightarrow 1-\alpha .
\]
If, in addition, a retained covariance estimator satisfies
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}-\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\]
with retained-rank pseudoinverse
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}
=\sum_{\widehat\lambda_{i,N}>t_N}
\widehat\lambda_{i,N}^{-1}
\widehat v_{i,N}\widehat v_{i,N}^{\top},
\]
and, under the auxiliary Gaussian conditional law given the retained record,
\[
Z_N^\ast\sim\mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}),\qquad
T_N^\ast=
(\Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z_N^\ast)^\top
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}(\Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z_N^\ast),
\]
then the conditional law of \(T_N^\ast\) converges in probability to the law
of \(T\).  This auxiliary convergence is a limit statement for the ideal
projected-Gaussian calibration law; it is not a realized finite comparison.
This is the only diagonal inference theorem in the main text.  Its confidence
language is conditional on \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}, on the
fixed candidate \(z_0=1/2\), and on the pre-specified reporting gate \(E_N\).
It is an ideal fixed-null asymptotic statement, not a simultaneous confidence
procedure over \(0<z<1\), not valid after data-dependent diagonal selection,
and not an executed finite-record accept/reject report.  The printed
\(z_0=1/2\) branch certificate supplies the population covariance branch
needed by the theorem; it is not a diagnostic pass, a model certificate, or a
critical-value transcript for a realized record.  The theorem does not supply
record-specific intervals \([Q_N]\) or \([L,U]\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The raw quotient CLT in the theorem statement and the local projection
expansion of \Cref{lem:local-projection-expansion-before-theorem-e-main} give the
projected limit at the fixed exchange diagonal:
\[
X_N
=\Pi_{H_{z_0}}\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_0)+o_{\PP}(1)
\Rightarrow
X:=\Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z .
\]
Here the projection lemma is used only at \(z_0=1/2\); no assertion is made for
a moving, selected, or simultaneous family of diagonal points.

The printed branch certificate gives full rank, so
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\) is invertible.  The continuous-mapping theorem yields
\[
X_N^\top\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}X_N\Rightarrow
T:=(\Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z)^\top
\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}(\Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z).
\]
Since the certificate establishes \(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=2\),
\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main} gives continuity of
the limiting distribution at its lower \((1-\alpha)\)-quantile and exact
probability content \(1-\alpha\).  Hence
\[
\PP_{\sigma_0}\{
X_N^\top\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}X_N\le c_{1-\alpha}\}\to1-\alpha .
\]
Finally, \(\PP_{\sigma_0}(E_N^c)\to0\) by
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.  Intersecting the preceding
event with \(E_N\) changes its probability by at most \(\PP_{\sigma_0}(E_N^c)\),
which proves the ideal fixed-null event.

For the optional auxiliary calibration-law convergence, Weyl's inequality and
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}-\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\]
the retained-rank rule keeps exactly the positive population eigenspaces with
probability tending to one.  The printed branch certificate gives full rank,
so on that event
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}\to \Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}
\qquad\text{in probability}.
\]
The conditional projected-Gaussian lemma
\Cref{lem:projected-gaussian-bootstrap-halfspace-main}, applied with the
fixed half-space \(H_{z_0}\), \(\widehat\Sigma_N=\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\),
and \(\Sigma=\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\), gives weak convergence in probability of
the conditional law of \(T_N^\ast\) to the law of \(T\).  Since the certificate
establishes \(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=2\),
\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main} gives continuity of
the limiting distribution at its lower quantiles.  This proves the stated
auxiliary convergence and deliberately stops before any record-specific
critical-value bracket or statistic interval is asserted.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Finite-record certificate interfaces.}
Theorem~E deliberately stops at ideal fixed-null asymptotics.  A realized
finite-record accept/reject/undecided output would require the separate
certificate interface
\Cref{thm:certified-diagonal-inference-main,def:finite-diagonal-report-main,prop:finite-diagonal-report-master-main}.
Those appendix protocols require the \(z_0=1/2\) branch certificate, a
record-specific covariance input, an outward-rounded statistic interval
\([Q_N]\), and either an outward-rounded ideal conditional quantile bracket
\([L,U]\) or the canonical one-sided DKW simulated critical value.  The present
article supplies only the branch certificate; it supplies neither \([L,U]\)
nor \([Q_N]\), so it asserts no realized finite diagonal decision.  The DKW
route is the conditional auxiliary-simulation domination of
\Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}; it is not finite-sample
record coverage under \(P_N\) and is combined with Theorem~E only on the
product space of records and independent projected-Gaussian simulations.
No finite decision is asserted for \(z\ne z_0\), for a data-selected \(z\), or
without an attached branch certificate and critical-value transcript.

\paragraph{Finite-record implementation protocols.}
The retained-record reporting gates, separated-batch covariance recipe,
quotient projection rule, and diagonal calibration protocol are implementation
material.  They are collected in
\Cref{app:finite-record-implementation-protocols-main} after the regenerative
tools; the theorem statements above use only the named reporting events and
certification conditions stated in the main text.
\begin{proof}[Proof of \Cref{prop:finite-record-inference-audit-detailed}]
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main} verifies the
exponential renewal tail required by the complete-cycle reduction.  Applying
\Cref{lem:retained-cycle-covariance-adjacent-gap-main} to the displayed reward
\(Y_j=U_j-\Rinc L_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\) gives Palm centering, one-gap dependence, the
endpoint/look-ahead deletion estimate, the renewal random-index replacement,
and the Gaussian limit with covariance \(\Sigma_r=T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main} applies the
master adjacent-gap covariance theorem to the same \(Y_j\), the common
complete-cycle set \(J_N\), and the plug-in rewards \(\widehat Y_j\) from
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}.  With
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), it gives
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(N^{-1/3}\right),
\]
which is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\) for \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\).

The preceding argument is under the stationary retained renewal law.  For a
finite hidden start, the retained word after the burn-in deletion is compared
to the stationary retained word by
\Cref{lem:finite-hidden-start-path-contraction-main}.  All complete-cycle
indices, reporting gates, and covariance batches are measurable functions of
that retained word, so the same probability limits transfer without changing
the estimator definitions.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-delta-detailed}]
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main} gives
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r)
\]
and the auxiliary total-space covariance rate for
\(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\).  The stable quotient hypotheses
\(r_0>0\) and \(\Delta_{\mathrm{inv}}=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\) put the population point in the \(C^1\)
domain of the pre-diagonal quotient rule
\Cref{prop:finite-record-pre-diagonal-quotient-rule-main}.  Applying that
proposition with \(\Rinch_N=\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) gives the
displayed Gaussian limit and the sandwich covariance formula for
\(\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\).  This is an ordinary quotient delta
method statement; no exchange-diagonal projected-Gaussian theorem is used.

For the constrained D-MAP population, the deterministic quotient inverse
identity in \Cref{thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality} gives
\(\Phi(\Rinc)=(p+s,ps)\).  If
\(\Delta_\sigma=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2>0\), the quadratic
\[
z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0
\]
has the two distinct roots \(\{p,s\}\subset(0,1)\).  Therefore the visible
record identifies the unordered survival-factor set.  For known design
interval \(\Delta\tau\), applying \(z\mapsto-\log z/\Delta\tau\) to each root
gives the unordered numerical rate set; without known \(\Delta\tau\), the
identified rate-scale object is only
\(\{-\log p,-\log s\}=\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\}\).
The separated-root branch thresholds in
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main} are eventually
retained on any compact separated-root neighbourhood, but the proof stops at
unordered roots: a visible record supplies no ordering of the two hidden
labels.  Ordered labelled-rate Wald reporting is therefore left to the separate
side-data convention \Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main} and the
side-data corollary, not to Theorem~D.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Inverse delta method and projected exchange-diagonal charts.}
The next propositions keep three operations distinct.  First, the population
quotient inverse is the map \(r\mapsto(p+s,ps)\).  Second, separated labelled
rates require \(\Delta_\sigma>0\) and the deterministic oracle branch map of
\Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}, or an estimated map with the
high-probability oracle-agreement condition stated there.  Third, a
finite-record implementation uses explicit denominator thresholds, projection
onto the feasible symmetric-coordinate set, and a no-label rule when the
empirical roots are not retained.

\begin{lemma}[Feasible symmetric-coordinate set and computable projection]\label{lem:feasible-set-computable-projection-main}
Let
\[
F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\}.
\]
For \(0\le t\le2\), set
\[
\ell_F(t)=
\begin{cases}
0,&0\le t\le1,\\
t-1,&1\le t\le2 .
\end{cases}
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:feasible-symmetric-coordinate-set-main}
F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):0\le\sigma_1\le2,\quad
\ell_F(\sigma_1)\le\sigma_2\le\sigma_1^2/4\}.
\end{equation}
For any \(v=(v_1,v_2)\in\mathbb R^2\), define
\[
c_v(t)
=\min\{\max\{v_2,\ell_F(t)\},\,t^2/4\},
\qquad 0\le t\le2,
\]
and
\[
q_v(t)=(t-v_1)^2+(c_v(t)-v_2)^2 .
\]
The deterministic projection used below is the one-dimensional rule
\begin{equation}\label{eq:computable-projection-rule-main}
t_v=\min\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{0\le t\le2}q_v(t),
\qquad
\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(v)=(t_v,c_v(t_v)).
\end{equation}
This rule is Borel measurable, is a Euclidean nearest-point projection onto
\(F\), and resolves global nonuniqueness by choosing the smallest minimizing
\(\sigma_1\).  On any neighbourhood where the Euclidean nearest point is
unique, \(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) agrees with the ordinary projection.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(t=x+y\).  The unit-square constraints imply \(0\le t\le2\).  Among
points in \([0,1]^2\) with sum \(t\), the product \(xy\) is maximized at
\(x=y=t/2\), giving \(xy\le t^2/4\).  The minimum is attained at the boundary:
it is \(0\) for \(0\le t\le1\), and \(t-1\) for \(1\le t\le2\), because then
one coordinate can be \(1\) and the other \(t-1\).  Conversely, every value
between these two endpoints is obtained by continuously moving \(x\) along the
line segment \(\{(x,t-x):0\le x,t-x\le1\}\).  This proves
\eqref{eq:feasible-symmetric-coordinate-set-main}.

For fixed first coordinate \(t\), the closest feasible second coordinate to
\(v_2\) is exactly the clipping \(c_v(t)\) to the closed interval
\([\ell_F(t),t^2/4]\).  Therefore minimizing the squared Euclidean distance
from \(v\) to \(F\) reduces to minimizing \(q_v\) over the compact interval
\([0,2]\).  The minimum exists because \(q_v\) is continuous.  Choosing the
smallest minimizer gives the displayed deterministic tie-break.  Since the
argmin graph of a continuous function on a compact metric space is closed, the
leftmost argmin selection is Borel measurable.  The selected point is a nearest
point by the preceding fixed-\(t\) reduction, and uniqueness makes the
tie-break irrelevant.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fixed-window error bound for projected plug-in word-law reconstructions]\label{lem:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-error-main}
Let
\[
F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}
=\{(x+y,xy):0<x<1,\ 0<y<1,\ x\ne y\}.
\]
Fix a compact set \(K\Subset F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\).  For
\(\sigma\in K\), let \(\PP_\sigma\) denote the stationary renewal law
obtained by the projected plug-in word-law reconstruction induced by
\Cref{lem:algorithmic-complete-law-reconstruction-main}: compute the two
unordered roots of \(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0\), form the corresponding
gap law \(g_k(\sigma)\), set
\(\rho_*(\sigma)=\{1+\sum_{k\ge0}k g_k(\sigma)\}^{-1}\), and then use the
renewal inclusion product and finite inclusion--exclusion.

For every finite window \(I\subset\mathbb Z\) there is a finite constant
\(C_I(K)\) such that, for all \(\sigma,\sigma'\in K\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-lipschitz-main}
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\PP_\sigma\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP_{\sigma'}\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)\,\|\sigma-\sigma'\| .
\end{equation}
Consequently, if the population point \(\sigma_0=\Phi(\Rinc)\) lies in
\(F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\) and the finite-record projected coordinate
\(\widetilde\sigma_N\) is retained in such a compact \(K\) with
\(\sigma_0\in K\), then the reconstructed projected plug-in word probabilities
satisfy the fixed-window deterministic bound
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-record-projected-word-error-bound-main}
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\widetilde\PP_N\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP_{\sigma_0}\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)\,\|\widetilde\sigma_N-\sigma_0\|.
\end{equation}
On a denominator-stable neighbourhood of \(\Rinc\), if projection is inactive and
\(\Phi\) has Lipschitz constant \(L_\Phi\), then the same event also gives
\[
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\widetilde\PP_N\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP_{\sigma_0}\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)L_\Phi\,\|\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc\|.
\]
In particular, under the finite-record CLT hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}, every fixed-window projected
plug-in word-law reconstruction has error \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) at separated
interior population points.  This is only a consistency and fixed-window error
statement for the plug-in projection; it is not a finite-sample uncertainty
band, confidence theorem, or critical-value construction, and no
word-probability confidence intervals are proved here.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Compactness in \(F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\) gives constants \(\eta>0\) and
\(\delta>0\) such that every root pair over \(K\) lies in
\([\eta,1-\eta]^2\) and has separation at least \(\delta\).  Therefore the
ordered roots
\[
z_-(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma_1-\sqrt{\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2}}2,\qquad
z_+(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma_1+\sqrt{\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2}}2
\]
are \(C^1\), with uniformly bounded derivatives on a neighbourhood of \(K\).
The logarithms \(\theta_\pm(\sigma)=-\log z_\pm(\sigma)\) are also \(C^1\)
there.  For each fixed \(k\), the unequal-root formula in
\eqref{eq:algorithmic-reconstruction-gap-main} is therefore \(C^1\) in
\(\sigma\) on that neighbourhood.

The derivative bounds are uniform for the infinite mean appearing in
\(\rho_*\).  Indeed \(z_\pm\le1-\eta\) on a neighbourhood of \(K\), and the
derivatives of \(z_\pm^k\) are bounded by a constant times
\((k+1)(1-\eta/2)^k\) after shrinking the neighbourhood.  Hence
\(\sum_{k\ge0} k g_k(\sigma)\) and its first derivatives converge uniformly
on \(K\).  Thus \(\rho_*(\sigma)\) is Lipschitz on \(K\).

If \(I=\emptyset\), the only word has probability \(1\) under every
\(\PP_\sigma\), so the claim is trivial.  Otherwise let
\(d_I=\max I-\min I\), with \(d_I=0\) for a one-point window.  The Palm
renewal mass \(u_n(\sigma)\), \(0\le n\le d_I\), is a finite polynomial in
\(g_0(\sigma),\ldots,g_{n-1}(\sigma)\), with \(u_0=1\).  Hence each
\(u_n\) needed by the inclusion products in the window is Lipschitz on
\(K\).  For a nonempty \(B=\{i_1<\cdots<i_m\}\subset I\), the inclusion
functional
\[
\rho_*(\sigma)\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}
u_{i_{\ell+1}-i_\ell}(\sigma)
\]
is therefore Lipschitz on \(K\), with a constant depending only on \(I\) and
\(K\).  The empty inclusion has constant value \(1\).  Finally, each word
probability is a finite signed sum of at most \(2^{|I|}\) such inclusion
functionals, by
\eqref{eq:algorithmic-reconstruction-word-main}.  Taking the maximum over the
finite set of \(S\subset I\) gives
\eqref{eq:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-lipschitz-main}.

The finite-record statement is obtained by substituting
\(\sigma'=\widetilde\sigma_N\) and \(\sigma=\sigma_0\) in
\eqref{eq:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-lipschitz-main}; the reported
\(\widetilde\PP_N\) is exactly \(\PP_{\widetilde\sigma_N}\) on the retained
projected plug-in word-law reconstruction branch.  On a denominator-stable neighbourhood the map
\(\Phi\) is \(C^1\), hence Lipschitz.  When projection is inactive,
\(\widetilde\sigma_N=\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\), and the final
display follows.  The \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) conclusion follows from
\(\sqrt N(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-\Rinc)=O_{\PP}(1)\) in
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main} and from the fact that
projection is eventually inactive with probability tending to one at
separated interior points.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Projected plug-in word-law reconstruction]\label{prop:finite-record-complete-law-renewal-consistency-main}
Let \(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) be the complete-cycle inclusion estimator,
let \(\Phi\) be the quotient inverse map, and let
\(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) be the feasible symmetric-coordinate projection.  Use the
finite-record reporting gate \(\mathcal E_N\) of
\eqref{eq:finite-record-reporting-event-main} and the deterministic thresholds
\(\varepsilon_N,\eta_N\) in \eqref{prot:finite-record-main}.  The projected
plug-in word-law reconstruction is the following partial finite-record
procedure.  On
\(\mathcal E_N\), define
\[
\widetilde\sigma_N
=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\{\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\},
\]
let \(\{ \widetilde z_{1,N},\widetilde z_{2,N}\}\) be the unordered roots of
\[
z^2-\widetilde\sigma_{1,N}z+\widetilde\sigma_{2,N}=0,
\]
and set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:complete-law-retained-event-main}
\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}
:=\mathcal E_N\cap
\{\widetilde z_{1,N},\widetilde z_{2,N}\in[\eta_N,1-\eta_N]\}.
\end{equation}
On \((\mathcal E_N^{\rm law})^c\), no projected plug-in word probabilities and
no finite word probabilities are formed.  On \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\), the
projected gap law
\(\widetilde g_{k,N}\) computed from
\Cref{lem:algorithmic-complete-law-reconstruction-main} is nonnegative,
normalized, and is the \(D\)-row path law of a stochastic triangular kernel:
\[
\widetilde g_{k,N}\ge0,\qquad
\sum_{k\ge0}\widetilde g_{k,N}=1 .
\]
Only on this same retained event define
\[
\widetilde\rho_N
=\rho_*(\widetilde\sigma_N)
=\left(1+\sum_{k\ge0}k\,\widetilde g_{k,N}\right)^{-1},
\]
let \(\widetilde u_{n,N}\) be the Palm renewal masses of this same projected
gap law, and define the finite-record law \(\widetilde\PP_N\) by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-record-renewal-consistent-inclusions-main}
\widetilde\PP_N(B\subset\varXi)
=\widetilde\rho_N
 \prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}\widetilde u_{i_{\ell+1}-i_\ell,N},
\qquad B=\{i_1<\cdots<i_m\}\ne\emptyset,
\end{equation}
with empty-set inclusion value \(1\), and then by finite inclusion--exclusion
for words.  This law is an exact stationary renewal law.  Equivalently, on
\(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\),
\[
(\widetilde\rho_N,\widetilde\sigma_N)\in
\mathcal M_{\rm ren}
:=\{(\rho_*(\sigma),\sigma):\sigma\in F\}.
\]
The empirical coordinate \(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) is used only to
construct the quotient coordinate and to test denominator stability; after
projection, the renewal intensity is \(\widetilde\rho_N\), not
\(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\).

Assume now that the population point is separated and interior:
\(\Phi(\Rinc)\in F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\), with the inverse denominators bounded
away from zero.  If
\(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\to \Rinc\) in probability, then the projected
coordinate satisfies \(\widetilde\sigma_N\to\Phi(\Rinc)\) and
\(\widetilde\rho_N\to r_0\) in probability on the retained branch, whose
probability tends to one for any thresholds tending to zero slowly enough.
For every fixed finite window \(I\) there are a compact separated-root
neighbourhood \(K\) of \(\Phi(\Rinc)\) and a constant \(C_I(K)<\infty\) such that,
with probability tending to one,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:complete-law-reporting-fixed-window-bound-main}
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\widetilde\PP_N\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)\,\|\widetilde\sigma_N-\Phi(\Rinc)\|.
\end{equation}
If the finite-record CLT hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main} hold, the right-hand side is
\(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Thus each fixed-window retained projected word
probability converges in probability to its population value with an explicit
projection-coordinate error bound.  The output is a projected plug-in word-law
reconstruction.  It is not a finite-sample confidence procedure for word
probabilities: no word-probability confidence intervals, bands, or
record-specific critical values are proved here.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On \((\mathcal E_N^{\rm law})^c\) the proposition asserts only the prescribed
withholding rule, so no renewal law or word-probability reconstruction has to be
interpreted there.  On the pre-specified event \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\), the
fixed-model construction evaluates the quotient inverse and first projects
the symmetric root coordinate into \(F\).  Since the projected roots lie in
\([\eta_N,1-\eta_N]\subset(0,1)\),
\Cref{lem:projected-root-gap-law-valid-main} applies to those roots and shows
that the quotient-root formulas are \(D\)-row path probabilities of a
stochastic triangular labelled kernel.  They therefore give a nonnegative
unit-mass gap law, hence a single Palm renewal law.  The intensity
\(\widetilde\rho_N\) is then defined from that same gap law by the renewal
identity \(1/\EE(G+1)\).  Therefore
\((\widetilde\rho_N,\widetilde\sigma_N)\) lies on \(\mathcal M_{\rm ren}\),
and the inclusion products are exactly the renewal products for the projected
finite-record renewal law.

At a separated interior population point, the inverse denominators are bounded
away from zero and \(\Phi(\Rinc)\) lies in the relative interior of \(F\).  The
complete-cycle inclusion estimator is consistent by
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}; hence
\(\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\to\Phi(\Rinc)\) in probability and
\(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) is eventually the identity with probability tending to
one.  The denominator thresholds in \(\mathcal E_N\) pass with probability
tending to one by \Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.  The two
population roots lie in \((0,1)\), while \(\eta_N\downarrow0\), so the
interior-root test in \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\) also passes with probability
tending to one.  Thus the conditional law-reporting event itself satisfies
\[
\PP\{(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law})^c\}\to0 .
\]
The
roots, gap probabilities for any fixed finite range, renewal masses for any
fixed finite range, and \(\rho_*(\sigma)\) are continuous on this separated
interior chart.  The quantitative fixed-window statement is exactly
\Cref{lem:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-error-main}, applied on a compact
separated-root neighbourhood of \(\Phi(\Rinc)\).  The displayed fixed-window
convergence follows from that bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Implementation note for projected plug-in word-law reconstruction]\label{rem:finite-record-complete-law-implementation-main}
The operational finite-record projection procedure is isolated in Algorithm~4
of \Cref{app:finite-record-implementation-protocols-main}.  That appendix
algorithm uses the retained event \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\) in
\eqref{eq:complete-law-retained-event-main}, the projected renewal law of
\Cref{prop:finite-record-complete-law-renewal-consistency-main}, and the
finite-window numerical tolerances of
\Cref{rem:complete-law-numerical-implementation-main}.  If any reporting gate,
denominator test, projection-root test, interior-root test, or declared
numerical certificate fails, no finite-record projected plug-in word
probabilities are formed.  The reconstruction contains projected word
probabilities only, not confidence intervals, bands, or critical values for
those word probabilities.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Symmetric-coordinate inverse delta method]\label{prop:finite-record-symmetric-coordinate-inverse-delta-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, and let
\(\Rinch_N\) be the all-complete-cycle inclusion estimator from
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}.  Suppose
\[
\sqrt N(\Rinch_N-\Rinc)\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r)
\]
with a reported covariance estimator \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\) whose auxiliary
total-space completion \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\) satisfies
the rate conclusion of \Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main}.  Assume the
population point lies in a stability neighbourhood where \(r_0\) and
\(|r_1/r_0-r_0|\) are bounded away from zero.  With \(\Phi\),
\(\widetilde\sigma_N\), and \(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\) defined by
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main},
\[
\sqrt N(\Phi(\Rinch_N)-\Phi(\Rinc))
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_{\sigma}),
\qquad
\Sigma_{\sigma}=D\Phi(\Rinc)\Sigma_rD\Phi(\Rinc)^\top .
\]
The same limit holds with \(\Phi(\Rinch_N)\) replaced by
\(\widetilde\sigma_N\) at separated interior points of \(F\), and the sandwich
\(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\) is consistent there.  For the constrained D-MAP
population, \(\Phi(\Rinc)=(p+s,ps)\).  If \(\Delta_\sigma=(p-s)^2>0\), the roots are
recovered as an unordered pair and the finite-record rule may report the
unordered numerical rate set only for known design interval \(\Delta\tau\);
otherwise it reports the unordered dimensionless product set
\(\{-\log p,-\log s\}\).  The ordered labelled-rate chart is not part of this
proposition; it requires the separate side-data hypotheses in
\Cref{cor:side-data-labelled-rate-wald-main}.
If \(\Delta_\sigma=0\), this proposition asserts only the symmetric-coordinate
delta method.  The diagonal no-label conclusion is the post-Theorem~E
corollary \Cref{cor:post-diagonal-no-label-reporting-main}, and the
common-rate estimand is handled by the projected exchange-diagonal statement.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the stated inverse stability neighbourhood, \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\).  The delta
method gives the displayed Gaussian limit for \(\Phi(\Rinch_N)\).  The
sandwich covariance inherits the same operator-norm rate because
\(D\Phi(\Rinch_N)\) is bounded and converges in probability to \(D\Phi(\Rinc)\)
on the denominator-retained event.  The identity \(\Phi(\Rinc)=(p+s,ps)\) is
exactly the quotient inverse calculation in
\Cref{thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality}.  At separated roots,
\(\Phi(\Rinc)\) lies in the relative interior of \(F\), so
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main} makes projection
asymptotically inactive.  Since
\(\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}\to\Delta_\sigma>0\) and
\((\delta_N^{\rm root})^2\to0\), the
discriminant threshold is eventually passed.  The empirical projected roots
converge to the two distinct population roots in \((0,1)\), so the endpoint
threshold is eventually passed, but the proof stops at the unordered root set
because no statistic of the visible record chooses the branch label.  At
\(\Delta_\sigma=0\), the proof stops at the symmetric-coordinate delta method;
the threshold/no-label statement uses the projected-coordinate limit from
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main} and is therefore deferred to
\Cref{cor:post-diagonal-no-label-reporting-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Post-diagonal no-label reporting on the exchange diagonal]\label{cor:post-diagonal-no-label-reporting-main}
Use the finite-record quotient/projection rule of
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main} and the
ideal fixed-null exchange-diagonal asymptotics of
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}.  In the submitted
manuscript this is the fixed certified point
\[
z_0=\frac12,\qquad \sigma_{z_0}=(1,1/4).
\]
At that point, the projected diagonal result of Theorem~E implies
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}).
\]
Consequently, for any deterministic threshold satisfying
\[
N^{1/2}(\delta_N^{\rm root})^2\to\infty,
\]
the separated-root labelled-rate chart is withheld with probability tending to
one:
\[
\PP\{\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}<(\delta_N^{\rm root})^2\}\to1 .
\]
This is only a no-label conclusion.  It does not remove the identified common
numerical rate
\[
R_{\rm diag}=-\frac{\log z}{\Delta\tau},
\]
whose coverage is the push-forward statement in
\Cref{cor:exchange-diagonal-common-rate-confidence-main}.
No corresponding coverage statement is asserted here for \(z\ne1/2\) without
a separate diagonal branch certificate and critical-value transcript.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
At the diagonal the local projected estimator satisfies the projected-Gaussian
limit in \Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}.  Therefore
\(\widetilde\sigma_N-\sigma_z=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  The discriminant map
\(\Delta(\sigma)=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2\) is locally Lipschitz near
\(\sigma_z\), and \(\Delta(\sigma_z)=0\); hence
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}
=\Delta(\widetilde\sigma_N)=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}).
\]
Because \(N^{1/2}(\delta_N^{\rm root})^2\to\infty\), the probability that
\(\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}\ge(\delta_N^{\rm root})^2\) tends to zero.  The
pre-diagonal rule then withholds separated labelled roots and labelled rates
with probability tending to one.  The common-rate statement is a separate
post-Theorem~E push-forward, namely
\Cref{cor:exchange-diagonal-common-rate-confidence-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite-record quotient projection and separated-label gate]\label{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main}
Let \(\Rinch_N\) be any three-coordinate finite-record estimator.  Let
\[
F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\},\qquad \sigma^\circ=(1,1/4),
\]
let \(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) be the computable tie-broken projection
\eqref{eq:computable-projection-rule-main}, and choose deterministic thresholds
\[
\varepsilon_N\downarrow0,\qquad \eta_N\downarrow0,\qquad
\delta_N^{\rm root}\downarrow0,\qquad
N^{1/2}(\delta_N^{\rm root})^2\to\infty .
\]
For every finite record, set
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}=\widehat r_{1,N}-\widehat r_{0,N}^2
\]
and define
\[
\widehat\sigma^{\rm raw}_N=
\begin{cases}
\Phi(\Rinch_N),
&\widehat r_{0,N}\ge\varepsilon_N,\quad |\widehat\Delta_{\mathrm{inv},N}|\ge\varepsilon_N,\\
\sigma^\circ,&\text{otherwise,}
\end{cases}
\qquad
\widetilde\sigma_N=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\widehat\sigma^{\rm raw}_N).
\]
For labelled separated-root reporting set
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}
=\widetilde\sigma_{1,N}^{\,2}-4\widetilde\sigma_{2,N}\ge0 .
\]
The separated-root branch is retained only when
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}\ge(\delta_N^{\rm root})^2
\]
and both projected roots of
\(z^2-\widetilde\sigma_{1,N}z+\widetilde\sigma_{2,N}=0\) lie in
\([\eta_N,1-\eta_N]\).  Labelled reporting also requires the deterministic
oracle separated-root branch map \(\mathcal B\) of
\Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}, or an estimated map used only on
the high-probability correctness event in the estimated-map reduction following
\Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}.  On this retained separated-root
event the rule records
\[
(\widehat p_N,\widehat s_N)
=\mathcal B(\mathcal R_{\mathrm{root}}(\widetilde\sigma_N)),
\qquad
(\widehat\Gamma_N,\widehat\kappa_{\mathrm r,N})
=\left(-\frac{\log\widehat p_N}{\Delta\tau},
       -\frac{\log\widehat s_N}{\Delta\tau}\right).
\]
Outside the retained separated-root event, the rule withholds separated
labelled roots and labelled rates.  At a true exchange-diagonal point, the
withholding probability tends to one by
\Cref{cor:post-diagonal-no-label-reporting-main}; away from the diagonal, the
rule is only the deterministic implementation of the separated chart supplied
by \Cref{prop:finite-record-pre-diagonal-quotient-rule-main}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The construction is deterministic after \(\Rinch_N\) is supplied.  If the
denominator thresholds fail, the internal placeholder \(\sigma^\circ\) only
makes the diagnostic map total within the fixed model contract.  On the
retained chart the rule computes
\(\Phi(\Rinch_N)\), projects it onto the feasible symmetric-coordinate set
by \Cref{lem:feasible-set-computable-projection-main}, and then applies only the
separated-root discriminant, endpoint, and branch-map tests.  Hence the rule is
well-defined for every finite record.  Its diagonal no-label probability is not
part of the pre-diagonal delta method; it is exactly the post-Theorem~E
conclusion in \Cref{cor:post-diagonal-no-label-reporting-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Diagonal projected-Gaussian exact branch]\label{cor:diagonal-projected-gaussian-exact-branch-main}
Use the notation and hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}.  This corollary is only a
cross-reference handle for the exact branch of Theorem~E.  In the submitted
manuscript it applies at the single certified point \(z_0=1/2\), where
\Cref{lem:z-half-diagonal-branch-certificate-main} proves
\(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)=2\).  Therefore, in the sense of the
retained-record convention \Cref{conv:boxed-retained-coverage-main},
\[
\PP_{\sigma_0}\{E_N\cap[
X_N^\top\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}X_N\le c_{1-\alpha}]\}\to1-\alpha .
\]
This is the ideal oracle-covariance event in Theorem~E, not a printed
finite-record statistic/critical-value comparison.
Additional diagonal candidates are not covered by this corollary unless a
future implementation extension supplies the printed certificates and
critical-value transcripts described in
\Cref{app:finite-domain-diagonal-certification-main}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main} is exactly the displayed
ideal fixed-null event, using the printed full-rank certificate and the
high-probability reporting gate.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Common-rate fixed-null pullback on the exchange diagonal]\label{cor:exchange-diagonal-common-rate-confidence-main}
Use the notation and hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}.
For the manuscript-certified candidate \(z_*=1/2\), set
\[
R_*=\frac{\log2}{\Delta\tau}.
\]
The paper-level common-rate auxiliary event is the pullback of the ideal
Theorem~E fixed-null event:
\[
\mathcal A_{R,N}(R_*)
:=\{E_N\cap[
X_N^\top\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)^{-1}X_N\le c_{1-\alpha}]\}.
\]
Thus, under the full-rank certificate printed in
\Cref{lem:z-half-diagonal-branch-certificate-main} and interpreted through
\Cref{conv:boxed-retained-coverage-main},
\[
\PP_{\sigma_0}\{\mathcal A_{R,N}(R_*)\}\to1-\alpha .
\]
The invariant ordered labelled-rate pair \((R_*,R_*)\) has the same
ideal fixed-null asymptotic event as Theorem~E.  This corollary is only a
deterministic reparametrization of the printed \(z_*=1/2\) theorem; it is not
an executed finite-record accept/reject report and it supplies no
record-specific statistic interval or critical-value bracket.  On \(\mathcal E_N^c\) any
common-rate display is withheld.  Any analogous pullback at another diagonal
point is an implementation extension and requires the additional printed
branch and critical-value certificates described in
\Cref{app:finite-domain-diagonal-certification-main}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The map \(z\mapsto-\log z/\Delta\tau\) is a bijection from \((0,1)\) to
\((0,\infty)\).  Therefore the true common rate \(R_*\) has the unique
preimage \(z_*=1/2\).  The displayed definition in the corollary makes the
common-rate event identical to the ideal Theorem~E event at \(z_*=1/2\).  The
stated coverage conclusion is therefore
\Cref{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Root splitting after the exchange-diagonal projection]\label{lem:exchange-diagonal-projection-main}
If \(y_N=\sigma_z+Z_N/\sqrt N+o_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) and
\(Z_N\Rightarrow Z\), then
\[
\sqrt N\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(y_N)-\sigma_z\}\Rightarrow \Pi_{H_z}(Z),
\qquad H_z=\{h:h_2\le zh_1\}.
\]
Let \(d_z=(1,z)^\top\) be the tangent direction to the exchange diagonal and
put
\[
V=\Pi_{H_z}(Z),\qquad
\Delta(v)=4(zv_1-v_2).
\]
If \(\PP\{V\notin\operatorname{span}(d_z)\}=1\), then for the unordered roots
\(\widehat z_{1,N},\widehat z_{2,N}\) of the projected polynomial
\[
t^2-\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(y_N)\}_1t+\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(y_N)\}_2=0
\]
one has the \(N^{-1/4}\) splitting law
\[
N^{1/4}|\widehat z_{1,N}-\widehat z_{2,N}|
\Rightarrow \sqrt{\Delta(V)} .
\]
More generally this convergence holds with the possibly degenerate right-hand
side \(\sqrt{\Delta(V)}\); on the event
\(V\in\operatorname{span}(d_z)\), the first-order discriminant vanishes and no
nondegenerate \(N^{-1/4}\) separation is asserted.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\Cref{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main} is the sole local
projection result used here.  Put
\[
H_N=\sqrt N(y_N-\sigma_z).
\]
Then \(H_N=Z_N+o_{\PP}(1)\Rightarrow Z\), hence \(H_N=O_{\PP}(1)\).  The
stochastic consequence in
\Cref{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main} gives
\[
\sqrt N\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(y_N)-\sigma_z\}
=\Pi_{H_z}H_N+o_{\PP}(1)\Rightarrow\Pi_{H_z}Z .
\]
Write
\[
\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(y_N)=\sigma_z+\frac{V_N}{\sqrt N}+o_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}),
\qquad V_N\Rightarrow V .
\]
For \(\sigma=\sigma_z+v/\sqrt N\), the polynomial discriminant is
\[
\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2
=\left(2z+\frac{v_1}{\sqrt N}\right)^2
 -4\left(z^2+\frac{v_2}{\sqrt N}\right)
=\frac{4(zv_1-v_2)}{\sqrt N}+\frac{v_1^2}{N}.
\]
The projection puts the limit in the tangent half-space
\(H_z=\{v:v_2\le zv_1\}\), hence \(\Delta(V)\ge0\).  Since the
distance between the two roots of a monic quadratic equals the square root of
its discriminant,
\[
N^{1/4}|\widehat z_{1,N}-\widehat z_{2,N}|
=\left(4(zV_{N,1}-V_{N,2})+o_{\PP}(1)\right)^{1/2}
\Rightarrow \sqrt{\Delta(V)}
\]
by the continuous mapping theorem.  The limit is nonzero exactly off the
tangent line \(v_2=zv_1\), which is
\(\operatorname{span}(d_z)\).  Thus the \(N^{-1/4}\) root separation is a
conditional nondegenerate statement, not a generic assertion made on tangent
directions.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Exchange-diagonal self-dual inverse]\label{cor:critical_self_dual_inverse}
At the exchange diagonal \(p=s\), the visible quotient inverse returns the
